The registry is looked up once, for the forward that is relayed; that forward is admitted with the received forward's acknowledgement, and the byte budget is charged with what the handler holds of both: each forward's plaintext and recipient kid list, and the thread id once (#98: a waiting forward is charged exactly what it holds). The relayed forward's not-before is the latest of the holds asked for by the received forward and by every opened layer, each capped and drawn at the receipt instant — never their sum, so a chain of layers is held at most the cap; its `expires_time` is the earliest of the received forward and every layer opened (FR-ROUTE-09). Whatever stops a layer the sender built from opening — its shape, its structure, a key the mediator does not hold, any other refusal of the unpack — is `Dropped-Malformed` (`Dropped-Expired` when the message inside has expired), never `Faulted`, which stays for faults of the mediator's own collaborators. The received forward is counted once, by how the chain ended (FR-ROUTE-11). (Present on `main` before #95 — `ForwardHandler` looked its own DID up and dropped such a forward, so a two-mediator route behind a rewrapping mediator never delivered; fixed toward the spec.) **Receiver side at the recipient — MUST (#96).** The last mediator of a route that rewraps hands the recipient a forward whose `next` is the recipient itself, so the recipient opens it: `PickupClient` (FR-PICK-11) unpacks every delivered message, fetched or pushed live, and opens a forward only when it has the shape the mediator's own rule above admits — exactly an anoncrypt envelope around a plaintext `forward`, no sender or signer key, no `from_prior`, exactly one attachment, and a `next` with the DID subject of the key that decrypted it (only that DID counts as this agent; another DID the agent holds does not). At most 8 such layers are opened per delivered message (so at most 9 unpacks), each with the full unpack rules (`MaxReceiveBytes`, FR-API-05 expiry, consistency); the message inside may be in any envelope, authcrypt included. A forward of any other shape, or a ninth layer, makes the delivered message a failed item: reported with its id, left queued, never handed over as a message. **Receiver side at a plain endpoint — MUST (#110, owner decision OD-3, 2026-11-22).** A rewrapping mediator that delivers to an agent's own endpoint (a push, ours or another implementation's) hands it the same forward addressed to itself. The question as put: "#110: in "rewrap" mode, the mediator wraps each message one extra time, addressed to the recipient. The spec (routing.md L71) says receivers must expect this. Our published plain receiving endpoint (MapDidCommEndpoint in DidComm.AspNetCore) doesn't unwrap it: it answers 202 and the message is never read. So a direct push in rewrap mode would get a message deleted unread. The same happens today with any rewrap-mode mediator delivering to our endpoint. What should #110 do?" The owner's answer: "Fix the endpoint in this PR (Recommended)". So every plain receive path of `DidComm.AspNetCore` — `MapDidCommEndpoint` (all three overloads) and `MapDidCommWebSocket` (both) — opens a `forward` addressed to the receiver itself, by the recipient's rule above (anoncrypted plaintext, no signer or `from_prior`, one attachment, `next` the DID that decrypted it), up to 8 layers, and hands on the message inside under its own envelope: who sent or signed it, or that it is anonymous, is that envelope's, never a layer's. Replay admission (FR-RR-08), its `created_time` rule and the 512-character id cap (FR-MSG-02) apply to the message inside, keyed on its own sender and id, and it never gets a return route (FR-RR-02). A ninth layer, or anything inside that is neither the message nor another such layer, is refused like an envelope that does not unpack (`400` over HTTP, discarded on a WebSocket), where it used to be answered `202` and the message inside never read; a message opened out of layers must also be in an envelope the endpoint accepts (`DidCommReceiveOptions.AcceptedMediaTypes`), judged by its own outermost envelope, not the wrapper's, or it is refused like a layer that cannot be opened (#110, O-3). Every other forward is handed on as it arrived: a plain endpoint relays nothing. A registry-aware endpoint whose dispatcher has a handler for forwards (the routing role, `AddRoutingMediator()` / `AddMediator()`) leaves a forward to that handler as it arrived (implementation choice, ours: opening a relay there would bypass `ForwardHandler`'s rules above), except a chain addressed to the mediator itself that ends in a message that is not a forward, which is the mediator's own (OD-6, above). A behaviour change in a published package. | Rewrapping produces a valid double-packaged message; rewrap resolves the DID subject of a `next` given with a fragment, a path or a query (`ForwardRewrapNextTests`). Receiver side: a forward addressed to the mediator itself is opened and the forward inside relayed; a DID URL of the decrypting DID counts as the mediator; another DID of the mediator is looked up and dropped as unknown; 2 and 8 nested layers are opened and a ninth is dropped as malformed without a registry lookup; inside, a message that is not a forward, one the mediator cannot decrypt, a payload that is not an envelope, and a plaintext layer are malformed, an expired forward is expired, an unregistered or non-DID `next` is unknown; two attachments on the self-addressed layer are malformed; opening happens on the request before anything is reserved, and a layer that cannot be opened reserves nothing; the forward inside has its delay drawn at receipt under the cap, and an outer hold that ends later is kept; no log line carries a DID, a kid or an exception object (`ForwardSelfAddressedTests`); a middle layer's hold is kept, the holds of several layers are never added, the earliest expiry of any layer (a middle one included) bounds the payload and a payload whose middle layer expires while queued is discarded on the pump; opening resolves no DID the sender chose, and an authcrypt layer, an anoncrypt of an authcrypt or of a signed message, an authcrypt of a signed message, a signed-only or plaintext layer and `from_prior` are refused as malformed before any DID is resolved, while a rewrapped layer still opens; an opened layer is written back as its packer wrote it, and refused while it is written once over the receive limit; whatever the depth, one request asks the registry once; a self-addressed forward is charged for the received and the opened forward and one byte over is dropped inline; a layer opened inline is never relayed when the request is aborted before its 202, and a request cancelled while a layer is opened leaves as cancellation with nothing reserved; a layer whose sender DID resolution fails in any way is a malformed drop logged by type names only (`ForwardPeelAdv3Tests`); every hostile shape of a self-addressed layer (bad base64, not UTF-8, not an envelope, a deeply nested JSON array, an over-long kid, two attachments, an inner `next` over the cap, an inner plaintext or signed-only forward — both now `Dropped-Malformed` — an extreme delay, an `expires_time` at the minimum) ends in one typed outcome, never `Faulted`; a nesting malformed at any layer never reaches the registry; sixteen nested layers cost at most 8 decryptions; a stranger's inner layer naming a slow DID holds up no other recipient's forwards, and nothing is reserved while it is opened; an inner layer signed by a DID that does not resolve is a malformed drop, not a fault; a mediator-service message inside a self-addressed forward is never executed by the forward handler itself; a forward naming the received layer's DID but decrypted by another held DID, and a `next` that only resembles the decrypting DID, are looked up, not peeled (`ForwardPeelAdvTests`); over the real endpoint a self-addressed forward is neither stored nor counted before its 202 is committed (it is opened inline, before the 202; the test `A_self_addressed_forward_is_not_relayed_before_its_202_is_committed` asserts exactly that nothing is stored or counted), an aborted request relays nothing, and every hostile forward is a 202 with one typed outcome (`MediatorPeelEndpointAdvTests`); a stranger's self-addressed forward around a signed note is opened on its own request (one lookup) as the mediator's message, holds up no other relay and stores nothing, and one sent on the mediator WebSocket is opened, relayed and answered with nothing (`MediatorEgressAdvTests`, `MediatorIdentityAndLogsAdvTests`); a captured `delivery-request` rewrapped inside a self-addressed forward is refused as a replay when the original was seen, and never answered on the wrapper's connection (`MediatorIdentityAndLogsAdvTests`); a two-mediator route delivers when the first mediator rewraps, the second picking the message up from the first's queue (`MediatorRoutesQaTests`); through a rewrapping mediator, a `recipient-update` is applied with nothing answered, a `messages-received` is applied, a `mediate-request` is applied and its grant logged, not sent; eight layers around a message for the mediator are opened and a ninth is dropped by the forward handler; a layer that is not a rewrap shape is never opened to the message inside; a forward for the next hop inside layers addressed to the mediator is relayed with every layer's expiry and delay; the message inside costs the same lookups of its sender's DID as when it arrives directly (`MediatorOwnMessageThroughRewrapTests`). Recipient side: a pass-through delivery opens no layer, a mediator's real rewrap output opens to the sender's message, 1, 2 and 8 nested layers open and a ninth is malformed; a DID URL of the decrypting DID counts as this agent; a `next` naming another DID (another DID the agent holds included), an authcrypt, signed-anoncrypt, signed-only or plaintext forward, `from_prior`, not exactly one attachment, an empty, non-base64url or data-less attachment, an expired layer or innermost message and a layer over `MaxReceiveBytes` are refused (`UnpackDeliveredTests`); with `RewrapMode.ReanoncryptToNext` the fetched and the live items are Alice's messages, a two-mediator route whose last hop rewraps delivers to Bob, and a relay layer for someone else is a failed item, not a message (`PickupClientRewrapTests`); sender-built self-addressed layers open up to eight and every hostile shape is a failure left queued (`PickupClientFetchAndRewrapQa2Tests`). Plain endpoint (#110): a message that is not a forward, and a forward that is not a layer re-wrapped for this agent, come back as received; a re-wrapped layer, the real rewrap output of a mediator and nested layers up to the bound are opened without unpacking the received layer again, and one more layer is malformed; a forward for another DID inside an opened layer and an attachment that is not a packed message are refused; an authcrypted message inside keeps its sender, an anoncrypted one stays anonymous, a signed one keeps its signer (`OpenReceivedRewrapTests`); a re-wrapped message reaches the consumer on every plain path, eight layers are opened and a ninth is refused with nothing handed on, the message inside is authenticated by its own envelope, the same message in two wrappers is admitted once, a stale message, an id over 512 characters and a tampered message inside are refused, a layer addressed to this agent around a message for someone else is not opened as ours, a forward not re-wrapped for this agent reaches the consumer as it arrived and is relayed nowhere, and with a handler registered for forwards a layer addressed to this agent is opened only to a message, never to a forward (`RewrappedReceiveTests`); a bad layer anywhere in the chain is refused like an envelope that does not unpack, and a message admitted on one path is refused on another in a different wrapper (`RewrappedReceiveQaTests`); an opened message whose own envelope the endpoint does not accept is refused, on the plain endpoints and on the mediator's (`RewrappedReceiveMediaTypeTests`); this library's mediator in rewrap mode pushes to a plain endpoint, which opens it for its handler, and its mailbox ends empty, while `MapDidCommMediator` still leaves a forward addressed to the mediator itself to its forward handler (`DirectPushRewrapEndToEndTests`). |
| FR-ROUTE-07 | MUST NOT | Do not honor `please_ack` on `forward` messages at a mediator. | Mediator ignores `please_ack`. |
| FR-ROUTE-08 | SHOULD | On transmission failure, try another endpoint or retry later (failover). | Failover attempted across multiple endpoints. |
| FR-ROUTE-09 | MUST | **Relay-time expiry.** A `forward` whose `expires_time` has already passed when the handler processes it, and a queued payload whose enclosing forward's `expires_time` passes before it is delivered, pushed or picked up, MUST be discarded and never delivered, regardless of the FR-API-05 reject-vs-warn setting. Expiry is judged with `DidCommOptions.Clock` and the same clock-skew allowance the unpack path uses for FR-API-05 (this is stricter than the spec's optional honoring and consistent with FR-API-05, which already refuses expired inbound at unpack). Only the forward's own header is enforceable: the inner payload's `expires_time` is encrypted and invisible to the mediator. The discard is logged with the forward's message id only — no DIDs, no payload. Where the discard happens: inline in `ForwardHandler` (on arrival), on its pump (expired while waiting behind the sink), in the mediator's forward sink when `MessagePickupQueue.DeliverAsync` finds it expired (not stored), and in the queue, which never hands out, reports or counts an expired record and removes it when its grant is next touched, when the store runs full, or at the mediator's scheduled purge (FR-PICK-10). For a forward found inside layers addressed to the mediator itself (FR-ROUTE-06), the relayed payload must not outlive any layer it came out of: its `expires_time` is the earliest of the received forward's and every opened layer's — checked inline when the layers are opened, again while it waits on the pump, and stored by the queue. Each such discard is counted once as `Dropped-Expired` on the `didcomm.forward.outcomes` counter; a forward that the unpack path already refused as expired (FR-API-05, the uniform 400) never reaches the handler and is not counted. | A forward with `expires_time` in the past that reaches the handler (dispatched directly; over HTTP the unpack path refuses it first with the uniform 400, FR-API-05 — `MediatorEndpointRelayTests`) is dropped: nothing is queued or sent, one `Dropped-Expired` is counted, and no log line carries a DID (`ForwardHandlerOutcomeMetricsTests`); one that expires while queued in the handler, or before the mediator's sink stores it, is dropped and counted the same way (`ForwardHandlerOutcomeMetricsTests`, `MediatorForwardSinkTests`); a queued payload whose expiry passes before `delivery-request` is not delivered, `status.message_count` excludes it, and the scheduled purge removes it on an injected clock (`MediatorHostedServiceTests`); the earliest expiry of every opened layer bounds the relayed payload, and a middle layer that expires while the acknowledgement is pending ends the relay (`ForwardSelfAddressedTests`, `ForwardPeelAdvTests`). |
| FR-ROUTE-10 | MAY | **`delay_milli` as a scheduling hint.** A mediator MAY honor `delay_milli` on a `forward`: a positive value is the earliest delivery time (`received + n` ms); a negative value means a uniformly random delay in `[0, \|n\|]` ms, drawn once at receipt. The result is stored as an absolute not-before time on the queued or pushed payload. A mediator that does not honor the hint MAY send a problem report but is not required to (spec §Routing). Honoring the hint never delays the transport acknowledgement (202 is returned immediately). **The honoured delay is capped** (owner decision of 2026-11-12): `ForwardProcessorOptions.MaxDelay` — set by `AddMediator()` from `MediatorOptions.MaxForwardDelay`, default **1 hour**, from 0 to `int.MaxValue` ms; `null`, the default for a bare `AddRoutingMediator()`, keeps the library's hard bound of `int.MaxValue` ms (about 24.8 days). The cap is applied in whole milliseconds (a fraction is dropped). A positive hint is held for `min(n, cap)`; for a negative hint `\|n\|` is clamped to the cap **before** the one uniform draw, which is then in `[0, min(\|n\|, cap)]` (clamping after the draw would put most of the probability exactly on the cap); a cap of 0 makes every forward due at once. No forward is refused because of its delay, and no problem report is sent. The not-before is the receipt reading plus the hold, pinned to `DateTimeOffset.MaxValue` when the sum would run past the end of time (a clock set close to it), so computing it never throws (before #95 the handler dropped such a forward as `Faulted` and the public overloads threw; present on `main` since #90). `ForwardHandler` and both public `ForwardProcessor.ProcessAsync` overloads apply it. The cap is also what bounds unseen quota occupation (FR-PICK-10). | `delay_milli: 500` → the payload is not deliverable (queue or live) before 500 ms on an injected clock; `delay_milli: -500` → the not-before time lies in `[now, now + 500 ms]` across repeated runs; the HTTP response is 202 before the delay elapses. With a 1-hour cap, hints of cap − 1 ms, cap and cap + 1 ms give a hold of 1 h − 1 ms, 1 h and 1 h, and `long.MaxValue` gives 1 h, through `ExtractDelay`, the handler's `NotBefore` and the public overloads; a negative hint of ten times the cap gives 1 000 draws all within `[0, cap]`, fewer than 1 % exactly at the cap and more than half of them distinct (a clamp after the draw would put about 90 % on the cap); `long.MinValue` never throws; a cap of 0 makes every hint due at once; a fractional cap is floored; `null` keeps the `int.MaxValue` ms bound; a `MaxDelay` outside `[0, int.MaxValue ms]` is refused by `Validate()` and by the processor (`ForwardDelayCapTests`); near the end of time the not-before saturates instead of throwing, through the handler and the public overloads, and a hold at the start of time is added normally (`ForwardDelayCapTests`, `ForwardPipelineQaTests`); `AddMediator()` takes the cap from `MaxForwardDelay` (`AddMediatorDiTests`). |
| FR-ROUTE-11 | MUST | **Forward as a registered protocol handler; silent drop of unroutable forwards.** `https://didcomm.org/routing/2.0/forward` is dispatched through the protocol handler registry like any other PIURI and is advertised with `roles: ["mediator"]` only when the mediator role is registered. A forward is relayed only when the outer envelope was encrypted and decrypted with a key the mediator holds (`RecipientKid` non-null) and `body.next` (a DID or DID URL, reduced to its DID subject per §4.3 `DidSubjectOf`) names a registered recipient — one known to the mediator's recipient registry: a Coordinate Mediation grant (FR-MED-05) or a host-registered recipient directory (sample 04) — or is the DID that decrypted the forward, which the mediator then opens and screens the forward inside (FR-ROUTE-06, receiver side). A forward that is plaintext, signed-only, or otherwise not decrypted by a held key, or whose `next` is unknown, or whose attachment is not a DIDComm message (FR-ROUTE-01; #98), is dropped and logged (id only) with 202; the attachment is checked at intake, before the registration lookup and before the forward is queued, charged to the byte budget or stored; the recipient lookup is constant-work with respect to registration state; no problem report is sent toward the sender (D4); the transport result is 202 in every case. The spec does not settle how a mediator answers an invalid forward (§Routing L25: a mediator MAY return a problem report for a header it doesn't support, the only answer Routing mentions; L27: `please_ack` on a forward MUST NOT be honored, to spare mediators sourceward communication; Problem Reports are general; §Transports L67: a successful receipt MUST be answered 2xx, failures are left open): this 202-and-drop answer is the owner's choice within that gap (D4, and A1 of 2026-11-19 for a payload that is not a DIDComm message), not a spec rule. `please_ack` stays ignored (FR-ROUTE-07). Because any stranger can cause a drop, each drop line is rate-limited per reason (the first and every 1000th at Warning — "expired while queued" at Information — the rest at Debug, same text). Every message id on the receive path is written bounded to 128 characters (a longer id as its length, "(id of N characters)"; one rule, `LogValues.Id`, for Core, `DidComm.AspNetCore`, `DidComm.Mediator` and `DidComm.Mediator.Client`), and no log message the library writes carries a stranger's message type (the spans' `didcomm.message.type` tag carries it only when it is at most 256 characters, all of them URI characters, and a placeholder otherwise; a host that copies span tags into its log scopes sees that tag value on the lines inside the span). Rate-limited the same way (first and every 1000th at their level, Debug otherwise): the forward handler's drop lines per reason, the mediator sink's drops, the endpoints' malformed-`return_route` and replay-refusal lines, and the dispatcher's FR-THR-04 rule-2 drop. Not rate-limited — one line per message: among others, the dispatcher's no-handler Information line, the endpoints' rejection lines for an envelope that cannot be unpacked (the HTTP "rejecting inbound envelope" Warning, the WebSocket "discarding … envelope" Warnings), the consumed pure-ACK Information line and the opt-in Trace 2.0 line; these log exception type names only, never the exception object or its text (which can quote wire values). In rewrap mode, a `next` whose DID resolves to no usable `keyAgreement` key or is of a refused method is `Dropped-Unknown` ("the next hop cannot be reached"); a resolver or key service that throws anything else is `Faulted`. and every forward that reaches the handler is counted once on the `didcomm.forward.outcomes` counter (tag `didcomm.forward.outcome`, a closed vocabulary, never a DID) by whichever stage ended it: the handler, or the mediator's sink. Under `AddMediator()`, `MapDidCommMediator` serves the forward through the same registry-aware HTTP endpoint (202 committed before the processor and the sink run; a 202 of the forward handler that the server has not taken within 10 s is withdrawn by aborting the request, which releases the forward's reservation and relays nothing, whether `next` is registered or not; the endpoint completes that 202 itself, so it is acknowledged once the server has taken it, a slow completion callback or a later client abort cannot withdraw it, and middleware that runs after the endpoint cannot change it; #98) and WebSocket endpoint as `MapDidCommEndpoint` / `MapDidCommWebSocket`. | discover-features discloses `routing/2.0` with `roles: ["mediator"]` only with the mediator role registered; a plaintext forward and a signed-only forward are dropped (202, nothing relayed, a log entry); unknown `next` → 202, nothing queued, no outbound message; the 202 is written before the forward sink runs (the sink is detached from the request), so response timing does not depend on registration state; the registry lookup itself is constant-work. Each drop path counts exactly one outcome; a sink that returns normally leaves the count to the sink and the mediator's sink counts one, so each forward has one outcome in total; the first and every 1000th line of a reason are Warnings, the rest Debug, each reason on its own count; no drop line carries a DID or an exception object (`ForwardHandlerOutcomeMetricsTests`). Through `MapDidCommMediator`: a forward is neither processed nor handed to the sink until its 202 has been committed, and one whose request is aborted before the 202 is committed never reaches the sink (`MapDidCommMediatorTests`); a forward whose attachment is not UTF-8 is refused at intake while its 202 is still held, with nothing reserved, charged or stored, `Dropped-Malformed`, and no DID in the logs (`NonDidCommPayloadTests`); an unknown `next` is answered 202 with nothing queued, pushed or reported, and a forward to a known and to an unknown `next` answer 202 with median response times within the FR-MED-08 timing-test tolerance (ratio < 3, interleaved batches over the real HTTP endpoint, run alone in a serial test collection) (`MediatorUnknownNextTests`, `MediatorUnknownNextQaTests`); status, headers, body and median time do not tell a known, a self-addressed, an over-cap and a non-DID `next` apart (`MediatorPeelEndpointAdvTests`); no drop line carries more than a bounded id (`ForwardPeelAdvTests`, `LogValuesTests`); no line the mediator's endpoints write for a stranger's rejected message carries its text (`MediatorIdentityAndLogsAdvTests`); a stranger's message type never reaches a log message, a 200 000-character id is written bounded on the dispatcher's and the admission's lines, the FR-THR-04 rule-2 drop line is a Warning at most the first time, and a rejected envelope quoting a forged line prints type names only on a console (`MediatorReceiveLogsAdv3Tests`); on a console host that copies activity tags into its scopes, a type of at most 256 URI characters reaches the lines inside the handle span and an over-long type or one with a line break or an escape never does (`MessageTypeConsoleScopeTests`, with the tag of every span in `MessageTypeSpanTagTests`); a stranger's own unresolvable recipient in rewrap mode is never counted as a mediator fault (`MediatorFaultedAdv3Tests`). |

### 8a. Return-Route and Queue Transport extension (FR-RR-*)

(Source: "DIDComm Messaging Return-Route and Queue Transport Extension", `decentralized-identity/didcomm-messaging` → `extensions/return_route/main.md`. It is an unversioned extension with no status line and no didcomm.org page; Coordinate Mediation 3.0 and Message Pickup 3.0 both state "The `return_route` extension must be supported by both agents". Placement (D1): header model in `DidComm.Core`, receive side in `DidComm.AspNetCore`, client side in `DidComm.Transports.*`. FR-TRN-03/10 are amended to defer to this section.)

| ID | Keyword | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| FR-RR-01 | MUST | **Header and values.** Recognize the plaintext header `return_route` with values `none` (default; an absent header means `none` for that message — "No messages should be returned over this connection" — on an established WebSocket session the session mode is unchanged, FR-RR-05), `all` ("Send all messages for this DID over the connection") and `thread` (send only messages matching the DID and the thread named in `return_route_thread`; the extension does not say where that attribute lives — it is read as a sibling top-level header, DD-12). An unrecognized or non-string value is treated as `none` and logged; `thread` without a usable `return_route_thread` — absent, not a string, longer than 2048 characters, or not a possible thread id (a `thid` is made of RFC 3986 unreserved characters, FR-MSG-02/11) — is treated as `none` and logged, and the value is never surfaced; it never fails unpack or dispatch. A `return_route_thread` of 513 to 2048 characters is still read, but no `thid` can match it (FR-MSG-11 bounds a `thid` at 512). Provide `MessageBuilder.WithReturnRoute(mode, thread?)` emitting the header exactly (`"return_route":"all"`). | Header parse table: absent → none; `none` / `all` / `thread` (+ `return_route_thread`) → the respective mode; garbage or non-string, and `thread` whose `return_route_thread` is absent, over-long or not made of unreserved characters → none with a log entry and the message still dispatched; the builder emits `"return_route":"all"` byte-exact. |
| FR-RR-02 | MUST | **Scope: direct peers only.** The header is honored only between the immediate sender and the agent it connects to directly ("not valid when routing keys are in use"): it is never honored on a `forward`, never propagated into a forward's payload, and never acted on by a relay — and never on a message an endpoint opened out of forward layers re-wrapped for it (FR-ROUTE-06): at a plain endpoint (#110, G-1) and at a mediator's endpoint for a message to the mediator itself (#110, OD-6). Such a message arrived on its mediator's connection, so it gets no session on any receive path, while replay admission, `created_time` and the id cap still apply to it. | An authcrypt `forward` carrying `return_route: all` opens no session and is relayed exactly as it would be without the header. A message opened out of re-wrapped layers gets no return route while the same message unwrapped gets its reply in band, and on a session socket it neither uses nor changes the session (`RewrappedReceiveTests`); on a WebSocket, and anoncrypted with `return_route` asked for, it opens no session (`RewrappedReceiveQaTests`); at a mediator, a `recipient-update` opened this way is applied and nothing is answered, and a `mediate-request`'s grant is logged, not sent (`MediatorOwnMessageThroughRewrapTests`). |
| FR-RR-03 | MUST | **Eligibility: authcrypt sender; session bound to that DID.** A return-route session is created only when the envelope is encrypted and contains an authcrypt layer (`authcrypt(…)`, `authcrypt(sign(…))` or `anoncrypt(authcrypt(…))`: the unpack result carries an authcrypt sender key id, `SenderKid` — `UnpackResult.Authenticated` alone does not suffice, because a verified signature also sets it; FR-CONSIST-05/06 satisfied) and the mode is `all` (`thread` is parsed and surfaced, FR-RR-01, but opens no session in this version: CM 3.0 and pickup 3.0 use only `all`; see §15.2); the session peer is `DidSubjectOf(SenderKid)`. Anoncrypt, signed-only, `anoncrypt(sign(…))` and plaintext inbound never open a session ("trust … MUST be associated with DIDComm encryption or signing, not from the socket connection itself"). Everything sent over the session MUST be addressed to exactly that peer (`to = [peer]`) and authcrypted from the DID whose key decrypted the inbound; a reply that would address anyone else is not sent. Live (WebSocket) sessions are registered and bounded, per DID and in total. The per-DID bound is applied first: a DID at its bound has its oldest session evicted to make room — also when the total bound is reached, because the total does not grow; only a registration that would grow the total is refused at the total bound, and another DID's session is never evicted. A message whose session was refused is still dispatched, without a session: nothing is written back for it on the socket (FR-RR-05). Evicting a WebSocket session closes the socket so the peer observes it. An HTTP session lives for one request (FR-RR-04): it is never registered, never counted against either bound, never evicted and raises no session notifications, so a burst of POSTs from a peer can never evict that peer's live WebSocket session. The eligibility and addressing rule is the single rule shared by HTTP and WebSocket and by the pre-existing `AllowSameSocketReplies` convenience (FR-TRN-10). | Eligibility matrix: anoncrypt / signed-only / `anoncrypt(sign)` / plaintext → no session; authcrypt and `authcrypt(sign)` → session with peer = sender DID subject; `anoncrypt(authcrypt)` → session; a message Mallory authcrypts with `return_route: all` can never receive Bob's traffic; a replayed message cannot open a session for another DID; registry bounds and eviction tested on WebSocket sessions; a peer at its own bound replaces its oldest session while the registry is full; at the total bound a new DID's message is dispatched without a session and no frame is written; a flood of fresh DIDs cannot evict an existing DID's session; HTTP `return_route: all` requests leave the registry count unchanged, raise no notification and never evict that DID's live WebSocket session. |
| FR-RR-04 | MUST | **HTTP binding (receive side).** On the ASP.NET Core receive endpoint, when a session exists and a handler produced a message, respond `200` with the packed message as the body and `Content-Type: application/didcomm-encrypted+json`; otherwise `202` with an empty body. At most one message is ever returned per request ("HTTP transports will only be able to receive at most one message at a time"); a further push on an HTTP session fails (`TrySendAsync` returns false) and the transport does not queue it. **Size-bounded send:** a session also offers `TrySendAsync(message, maxPackedBytes)` → `Sent` / `NotSent` / `TooLarge`: the bound is compared with the packed envelope — the bytes the response would carry, measured after packing, not estimated — and a message over it is neither held nor sent while the slot it claimed is given back, so a smaller message can follow (used by message-pickup deliveries, FR-PICK-03). The 400 / 413 / 415 paths and the rejection-timing floor are unchanged. The 200-vs-202 distinction is observable only by the authenticated sender, by design. No separate switch: an authcrypted `return_route: all` request (FR-RR-03) is honored on HTTP by default (the response is addressed only to the authenticated sender). | POST an authcrypt `ping` with `return_route: all` → 200 with the exact media type and a body that unpacks to an authenticated `ping-response` addressed to the sender; the same POST without the header → 202 empty (existing tests unchanged); a second push on the same HTTP session returns false; a push over its size bound returns `TooLarge`, holds nothing, and a following push within the bound is the 200 body; a bound below 1 is an `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. |
| FR-RR-05 | MUST | **WebSocket binding (receive side).** A WebSocket connection becomes a session at its first eligible message (FR-RR-03, admitted under FR-RR-08) and lasts for the connection lifetime unless the registry evicts it (FR-RR-03); the session mode changes only when a later message from the session's own peer on that connection carries `return_route`; a header-less message leaves the session mode unchanged (CM 3.0: the header is set "once per established websocket", so `all` asserted once is sufficient). A later `none` downgrades the session — nothing more is pushed until a later `all` resumes it — and so do `thread` (this version opens no thread-scoped session, FR-RR-03, and pushing everything would exceed what the peer asked for; §15.2) and a malformed value, which FR-RR-01 reads as `none`. On HTTP each POST is its own connection, so an absent header is `none` (FR-RR-01). Multiple messages may be pushed, each as one WebSocket message (FR-TRN-09), through a single writer so frames never interleave; nothing is pushed after the receive side has processed a downgrade or a close (a frame already being written completes). Pending pushes are bounded per session (host-configurable, default 16, plus the one being written): capacity is reserved before a push is packed, a push beyond the bound is refused at once (`TrySendAsync` returns false) without being packed, and a push never waits on the socket. On close the session is unregistered and its own `Closed` signal fires — the authoritative, prompt per-session signal (pickup live mode resets on it, FR-PICK-06); the registry's `SessionOpened` / `SessionClosed` notifications are best-effort: never raised inline in registration or unregistration, delivered one at a time in the order of the state changes by one notifier per registry, and bounded (host-configurable, default 1024 waiting) — under subscriber backpressure the oldest are dropped and counted — so a subscriber can never delay a registration, an eviction, a lookup or the closing of an evicted session, and a blocked one holds at most one thread. Two sockets for one DID are two sessions. Enabled by the existing `AllowSameSocketReplies` switch (default off); not available under STOMP. The mediator composition (`MapDidCommMediator`, §12 Phase 7) turns same-socket replies on for its own WebSocket endpoint only, and always uses bare framing there (STOMP off, since a STOMP connection can never carry a reply); the host's other WebSocket endpoints keep the configured switch. The socket's peer is fixed by its first eligible message — also when the total bound refused that session, in which case only that peer may open it with a later eligible message — and a session that has closed is never reopened on its socket; a message authenticated by another DID on the same socket is dispatched without a session: it neither opens, re-binds nor changes the session, and its reply is not written. The size-bounded send of FR-RR-04 applies to a WebSocket session too: a packed frame over the caller's bound is not queued and the push slot it reserved is given back. **As built (#100, OD-8):** a session never outlives its connection. It closes the moment the connection's token is cancelled (the peer aborted, a broken socket, the eviction backstop), also while the receive loop still finishes the message it had received in full (FR-TRN-14), so a reply or a push made meanwhile is refused quietly (`false`) and nothing is written. A connection that has already ended opens no session, so its message in hand can neither take a place in the registry nor evict a live session of the same peer. | Two handler pushes arrive on the same socket in order; close → registry empty, the session's `Closed` signalled and `SessionClosed` raised after the unregistration; a subscriber that blocks in `SessionOpened` or `SessionClosed` under reconnect churn keeps the outstanding notifications within the bound and delays neither a registration nor the close of an evicted socket; a subscriber that keeps up receives every notification once, in state-change order; a second socket for the same DID → two sessions; a later header-less message on the socket does not stop pushes; a later `return_route: none`, `thread` or malformed value from the session's peer does, and a later `all` resumes them; pushes racing a `none` are refused from the moment it is processed; with the writer blocked, pushes beyond the pending bound return false at once and are never packed; another DID's message on the session socket is dispatched and its reply is not written; STOMP → no session and no push. A push over its size bound returns `TooLarge`, writes nothing and leaks no push slot (`MaxPendingPushes + 1` such pushes are followed by a successful one); at the exact packed size it is sent, one byte below it is not. As built (#100, OD-8): a push and a reply made after the drop are refused quietly, and a connection that ended opens no session, so its message in hand cannot evict the peer's live session (`WebSocketMessageFinishTests`). |
| FR-RR-06 | MUST | **Queue transport URI.** `didcomm:transport/queue` is recognized as a `DIDCommMessaging` `serviceEndpoint.uri` meaning "messages are held at the sender for pickup by the recipient". Parse-and-recognize only: no transport is registered for it, and an attempt to send to such an endpoint — resolved from the recipient's service entry or supplied as an explicit endpoint override — fails before any transport activity with a `TransportException` whose text names the queue URI and says the sender holds the message for pickup (return-route / message-pickup), and does not echo the DID. A resolved endpoint is known only once the recipient's DID is resolved and the message packed, because the endpoint lives in the service entry; that resolution may itself use the network (e.g. `did:webvh`), but nothing is handed to a transport. | A DID document whose service `uri` is `didcomm:transport/queue` parses; `SendAsync` to it — resolved, or as an endpoint override — throws `TransportException` before any transport activity (no transport is called), and the exception text carries no DID. |
| FR-RR-07 | MUST | **Client side.** `SendOptions.ReturnRoute` is nullable: `null` (the default) emits no header and leaves any `return_route` the builder already set (FR-RR-01) untouched; `ReturnRouteMode.None` emits `"return_route":"none"` explicitly; `All` and `Thread` (+ `ReturnRouteThread`) emit those values — stamped before packing, and a non-`null` `SendOptions.ReturnRoute` overrides a builder-set header. Everywhere below, "a send with `ReturnRoute: All`/`Thread`" means the effective header after this step. `ReturnRoute` of `All` or `Thread` is refused with `InvalidOperationException` once the recipient's service entry is read and the route would contain at least one forward layer (`routingKeys` non-empty or `uri` is a DID) — before any mediator or routing-key DID is resolved and before packing (FR-RR-02); a direct recipient (plain `uri`, no `routingKeys`) is allowed even though `SendAsync` packs with `Forward: true` by default, because that route has zero forward layers. HTTP: a `200` response whose content type is `application/didcomm-*+json` is read into `TransportResult.ResponsePayload`, capped at `DidCommOptions.MaxReceiveBytes` (an over-cap body is never buffered: the transport reports the send accepted with no payload and `ResponseDiscarded`, and logs it; `SendAsync`, having asked for the return route, then throws `TransportException` with status 200, the same outcome as an unpack failure; a body that stalls past the transport's request timeout or fails mid-read is treated the same); `202` or a non-DIDComm content type yields no payload. The facade unpacks the payload into `SendResult.Returned` (no dispatch — the caller asked for it) only when the response unpacks as encrypted **and** authenticated and `DidSubjectOf(SenderKid)` equals the DID the request was addressed to; anything else is discarded and logged (the send still counts as accepted). The same rule applies to messages the WebSocket inbound loop dispatches. An unpack failure surfaces as `TransportException`, never a raw parser exception (FR-API-07 parity). WebSocket: an opt-in inbound receive loop (`ReceiveInbound`, default off) surfaces pushed messages, size-capped, to the protocol dispatcher. **Connection affinity (WebSocket).** Affinity is separate from header emission. A send with `ReturnRoute: All` (or `Thread`) from local DID L to peer P at endpoint E opens, or reuses, the session socket S(E, L, P), selected by (endpoint, local sender DID, remote peer DID) and never by endpoint alone, because the receive side binds a socket to its first authenticated sender (FR-RR-05). While S exists, **every** message from L to P at E uses S regardless of the header: `null` (omitted) preserves the session — the receive side leaves the mode unchanged (FR-RR-05), which is how CM 3.0's once-per-socket `all` and a headerless Pickup 4.0 `messages-received` reach the session and travel over it; an explicit `None` is sent **on S** so the receiver downgrades it (FR-RR-05), after which the client retires S once the send completes (a graceful close that drains frames already received; the receiver pushes nothing after it processes the `None`), and later sends from L to P use the shared endpoint-keyed pool until a new `All` opens a fresh session socket. Traffic with no session for (E, L, P) — unrelated one-way application sends — keeps the shared endpoint-keyed pool. Pushed or returned messages arriving on S are unpacked for, and attributed to, L. | Header stamped exactly `"return_route":"all"`; a routed recipient + `ReturnRoute` → `InvalidOperationException`, no mediator DID resolved, nothing packed; a direct recipient + `ReturnRoute` succeeds; 200 + DIDComm type → `Returned` is the authenticated reply; 202 → null; an anoncrypt body, or one authenticated by a different DID, under a DIDComm content type → `Returned` is null and a log entry; a 100 MB body is cut at the cap without being allocated; garbage under a DIDComm content type → wrapped `TransportException`; a WS push reaches a registered test handler and a socket drop ends the loop with no unobserved exception; two local DIDs enrolling through one `WebSocketDidCommTransport` instance at one mediator endpoint with `ReturnRoute: All` use two distinct sockets and each receives its own grant (`to` = its own DID, `thid` = its request); with `ReturnRoute` unset (no session) both share one pooled socket; on one session socket the sequence `All` → headerless request → explicit `None` yields the headerless request's reply over that same socket, no push after `None`, and a later `All` opens a new socket; a headerless Pickup 4.0 `messages-received` sent after live deliveries travels over the session socket and the pushes continue. |
| FR-RR-08 | MUST | **Replay.** Replay protection and the `created_time` admission rule below apply to exactly two classes of inbound, both carrying an authcrypt layer (FR-RR-03: an authcrypt sender key id — a verified signature alone does not count): **(a)** a message that carries `return_route` other than `none` (a malformed value is `none`, FR-RR-01), and **(b)** a message whose `type` is under a mediator-service PIURI (`https://didcomm.org/coordinate-mediation/2.0`, `https://didcomm.org/coordinate-mediation/3.0`, `https://didcomm.org/messagepickup/3.0`, `https://didcomm.org/message-pickup/4.0`) — the messages that can open a session or mutate mediator state. The replay key's peer is the DID subject of that authcrypt sender key id, never the plaintext `from`. A message that is not admitted is not dispatched and opens or changes no session; HTTP answers it `202` with an empty body and a WebSocket writes nothing. The rule applies on every receive endpoint that dispatches to protocol handlers or hands the host a return-route session, and one cache serves all of a host's endpoints, so a request admitted over HTTP is a duplicate over WebSocket and vice versa; the pre-existing callback-only endpoints, which can open no session and host no mediator-service handler, pass unpacked messages to the host's callback unchanged. **Ordinary application traffic is exempt:** a message without `return_route` whose `type` is outside those PIURIs is dispatched as today with only FR-API-05 expiry applied, so a timestamp-free or delayed application message is never rejected by this rule; `forward` (routing/2.0) is exempt likewise (anoncrypt, FR-ROUTE-11). Plaintext, anoncrypt, signed-only and `anoncrypt(sign(…))` messages are outside the scope even when they carry `return_route` or a mediator-service type: they pass replay admission and are dispatched, can open no session (FR-RR-03), and can mutate no mediator state because the mediator-service handlers drop them (FR-MED-05, FR-PICK-01). (1) **Freshness window.** A request is admitted only if `created_time` is present and lies in `[now − replay_window, now + replay_clock_skew]`, in whole seconds, where `replay_window` (default 5 minutes) and `replay_clock_skew` (default 60 seconds, zero or more; `ReplayProtectionOptions.Window` / `ClockSkew`) are host-configurable; a request older than the window, or later than the skew allows, is dropped and logged (id only) with 202, exactly like a duplicate. `replay_clock_skew` is the replay rule's own tolerance for a peer whose clock runs ahead; it is independent of the FR-API-05 expiry tolerance (`DidCommOptions.ExpiresClockSkew`, default 0), which governs expiry only and never widens this window, and widening it never lets an expired message through. The same setting applies wherever the rule runs — the receive endpoints and the client's WebSocket inbound loop (FR-RR-07) — and one container has one value for both; an HTTP `SendResult.Returned` is not replay-admitted (FR-RR-07), so no replay setting decides it. (2) **Timestamp-free requests.** By default (`require_created_time = true`, applying to the scope above and nothing else) a request without `created_time` is dropped and logged with 202. A host MAY set `require_created_time = false`; such a request is then admitted once and its id retained for `replay_window`, after which the identical captured request is accepted again — a stated limitation of that configuration, not a guarantee of this row. (3) **Atomic admission.** Admission is an atomic check-and-insert on the key (peer DID subject, `id`; the subject compared exactly, the `id` ignoring case per FR-MSG-04, since #141) under one lock or lock stripe for that key, so concurrent duplicates admit exactly one. (4) **Retention.** An entry is retained until at least `created_time + replay_window` — the instant from which the same request is rejected on age (`created_time < now − replay_window`) — and in practice until `created_time + replay_window + replay_clock_skew`, an over-retention that costs budget, never integrity (a request stamped `now + replay_clock_skew`, the latest admitted, holds its entry `replay_window + 2 × replay_clock_skew`); entries are never evicted earlier, so no gap exists between cache retention and age rejection. The gap stays closed whatever order clock readings reach the cache in — two requests whose readings arrive out of order, or a host clock that steps back: a request whose entry would already have expired at the latest instant the cache purged at (`created_time + replay_window + replay_clock_skew` earlier than it) is refused on age, because it may have been forgotten; with readings in order this is the ordinary age rule. The price is availability, never integrity: a reading D seconds behind that instant refuses in-window requests older than `replay_window + replay_clock_skew − D`, so after the host clock steps back by more than `replay_window + replay_clock_skew`, even fresh in-scope requests are refused until the clock is back within that span of the latest purge instant (an in-memory cache also starts over when the process restarts), and the cache logs such refusals. The cache's settings are fixed when it is built. (5) **Budget.** The cache has a global entry budget (host-configurable) covering all peers; per-peer buckets are bounded and the number of buckets is bounded by the same global budget, so fresh DIDs cannot grow it without limit. When the budget is saturated a new (peer, `id`) that cannot be recorded is **not admitted** (dropped, logged, 202, a metric) — admitting unrecorded requests would reopen replay; admission resumes as entries age out. Saturation therefore degrades availability, never integrity, and the budget is sized to the endpoint's accepted rate × (`replay_window` + `replay_clock_skew`) — a flood stamped at the future bound holds it for `replay_window + 2 × replay_clock_skew`. An in-scope message whose `id` exceeds a host-configurable length cap (default 512 characters) is not admitted either (dropped, logged, 202), so an oversized id can never be stored budget-many times. Unpack already refuses an `id` over 512 characters (FR-MSG-02), so on the endpoints and the client's inbound consumer a cap above 512 has no effect; a lower one still applies. A replayed request re-executes nothing (`recipient-update`, `messages-received`, `mediate-request`) and opens no session; the session holder is the connection that presented the admitted message, and confidentiality rests on `to = [peer]` authcrypt, not on session ownership. | Immediate replay → 202, handler invoked once in total, no second session. Clock advancement (injected clock): a replay just inside `replay_window` is rejected by the cache; the clock is advanced past `created_time + replay_window + replay_clock_skew` and the same request is rejected on age; an entry is still present until that instant. Clock skew: with default options `created_time = now + 59 s` and `now + 60 s` are admitted and `now + 61 s` is dropped (202, handler not invoked); `replay_clock_skew = 0` drops `now + 1 s`; a request admitted with a future `created_time` is refused at every instant — a duplicate until `created_time + replay_window`, too old after — and its entry is held until `created_time + replay_window + replay_clock_skew`; a clock reading that reaches the cache after a later one, and a clock stepped back after a purge, never re-admit a replay; `ExpiresClockSkew` alone changes no admission, and an `expires_time` one second in the past is rejected with default options whatever `replay_clock_skew` is; a negative `replay_clock_skew` fails validation; two hosts whose clocks are 30 s apart complete ping with `return_route: all`, enrolment and pickup in both directions over HTTP and WebSocket. Timestamp-free request → dropped by default; with `require_created_time = false` it is admitted once, rejected within the window, and admitted again after it (the documented limitation). N concurrent POSTs of one message → exactly one handler invocation; a request admitted over HTTP is dropped when replayed over the host's WebSocket endpoint. Capacity: with the global budget full, a new message from a new peer is dropped with 202 and no existing entry is evicted; after entries age out it is admitted. Fresh-DID flood: bucket count never exceeds the budget and existing peers' entries survive. Scope: a timestamp-free trust-ping without `return_route` is dispatched normally; the same ping with `return_route: all` and no `created_time` → 202, dropped, logged; a timestamp-free authcrypt `status-request` (no `return_route`) → dropped; a signed-only or `anoncrypt(sign)` message carrying `return_route: all` without `created_time`, and an unauthcrypted pickup request, pass admission and reach their handlers with no session; an in-scope message whose `id` is over the cap → dropped; a 10-minute-old application message without `return_route` is subject to FR-API-05 only and reaches its handler. |
| FR-RR-09 | MUST | **Client timestamps.** Every outgoing message that falls under the FR-RR-08 scope carries `created_time`. `DidCommClient.SendAsync` stamps `created_time` from `DidCommOptions.Clock` — the same clock FR-RR-08 and FR-API-05 use — whenever the outgoing plaintext's `return_route` header is `all` or `thread` after `SendOptions.ReturnRoute` is applied (whether it came from `SendOptions` or from `MessageBuilder.WithReturnRoute`, FR-RR-01), or the message `type` is under a mediator-service PIURI, and the message has no `created_time` yet. `MediationClient` (FR-MED-09), `PickupClient` (FR-PICK-11) and the Discover Features bootstrap query (FR-MED-09) therefore need no caller action; acknowledgements are included (`messages-received`, which under Pickup 4.0 carries no `return_route` and is caught by the PIURI clause). A `created_time` the caller supplied is **preserved, never refreshed**: a stale request is sent as-is and rejected by the receiver (FR-RR-08), not laundered into a fresh one. `MessageBuilder.WithCreatedTime` stays the explicit path; `TrustPing.CreatePing` and `DiscoverFeatures.CreateQuery` are unchanged (stamping happens at send). Messages outside the scope are sent without a `created_time` being added. | Default client against default server with no caller-supplied timestamps: a trust-ping with `ReturnRoute: All` over HTTP → 200 `ping-response` (FR-RR-04); discover-features → `mediate-request` → `recipient-update` → `delivery-request` → `messages-received` → `status` 0 succeeds over HTTP and over WebSocket; a request built with an explicit `created_time` 10 minutes old is sent with that value unchanged and gets 202 with no reply; an application message without `return_route` is sent with no `created_time` added; the stamped value equals the injected clock; a message carrying `return_route: all` via `MessageBuilder.WithReturnRoute` with `SendOptions.ReturnRoute` unset is stamped and, over WebSocket, uses the session socket. With the library's own clients (#96), end to end against the #95 mediator: discover-features → `mediate-request` → `recipient-update` over HTTP, over a WebSocket named by the mediator's DID and over an `http` DID with a `ws` override, every request with `return_route: all` and `created_time` = the client's injected clock, deliberately 20 s apart from the mediator's (`MediatorClientEnrolmentQa1Tests.Acceptance_flow_negotiate_enrol_publish_register_and_Alices_forward_reaches_the_queue_of_that_DID`; the five requests of an enrolment over HTTP and WebSocket, `MediatorClientEnrolmentTests`); `delivery-request` → `messages-received` → `status` 0 over HTTP and over WebSocket, live mode included, every pickup request stamped from the client's clock (`PickupClientHttpEndToEndTests.Enrol_send_fetch_and_acknowledge_then_the_status_is_zero`, `PickupClientWebSocketEndToEndTests`, `PickupClientAcceptanceQa2Tests`); the request handed to the client's channel is never stamped by hand or modified (`MediatorClientChannelEndToEndTests.Over_HTTP_the_answer_is_the_response_body_and_every_request_is_stamped_from_the_injected_clock`). |

### 8b. Coordinate Mediation (FR-MED-*)

(Source: https://didcomm.org/coordinate-mediation/3.0/, status Production; PIURI `https://didcomm.org/coordinate-mediation/3.0`; roles `mediator`, `recipient`; "No protocol specific errors exist." Package: `DidComm.Mediator` for the mediator role (D1); the recipient role and the builders and parsers both roles use are in `DidComm.Mediator.Client` (#113). The protocol page is silent on threading and on proof of DID ownership; DD-11 and DD-14 record the choices.)

| ID | Keyword | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| FR-MED-01 | MUST | **Protocol identity and advertisement.** Implement `https://didcomm.org/coordinate-mediation/3.0` with roles `mediator` and `recipient`. A host that registers the mediator role discloses the PIURI with `roles: ["mediator"]` through Discover Features 2.0 (FR-PROTO-05); an ordinary agent does not. | A discover-features `protocol` query matching `https://didcomm.org/coordinate-mediation/*` discloses 3.0 with `roles: ["mediator"]` only when the mediator role is registered. |
| FR-MED-02 | MUST | **Message shapes (exact).** `mediate-request` (`body: {}`) → `mediate-grant` `{"routing_did": [string, …]}` or `mediate-deny` (`body: {}`) — for `mediate-request` / `mediate-deny` emit `body: {}`; an absent `body` is accepted on receive as a tolerance (the didcomm.org examples omit it); `recipient-update` `{"updates": [{"recipient_did", "action": "add" \| "remove"}]}` → `recipient-update-response` `{"updated": [{"recipient_did", "action", "result": "client_error" \| "server_error" \| "no_change" \| "success"}]}` with entries in input order; `recipient-query` `{"paginate"?: {"limit", "offset"}}` ("if present must include `limit` and `offset`") → `recipient` `{"dids": [{"recipient_did"}], "pagination"?: {"count", "offset", "remaining"}}`. Parsers reject an unknown `action` or `result`, a non-array `updates`, and a `paginate` missing `limit` or `offset` with the existing `MalformedMessageException` / `ProtocolException` types and no echo of wire values. Members the protocol does not define are ignored, in `body` and in each entry (so `mediate-request` / `mediate-deny`, which define none, accept any `body`); an explicit `null` for the optional `paginate` / `pagination` reads as absent. Message types are matched as the handler registry matches them (FR-PROTO-01/02): any 3.x minor, ignoring case and `-` / `_`. A `mediate-grant` whose `routing_did` is a string (the 2.0 shape) is accepted on parse; the library always emits the array in 3.0 (a 2.0 grant writes a single string, FR-MED-10). | Byte-exact round trips against checked-in fixtures derived from the didcomm.org 3.0 page examples and normalized to valid JSON; a rejection table covering each malformed shape; string `routing_did` accepted, array emitted. |
| FR-MED-03 | MUST | **Return-route and transport.** Recipient requests carry `return_route: all` — on every HTTP POST and once per WebSocket — and the mediator's replies go back over the session (FR-RR-04/05). A request without the header is still answered: the handler produces the reply and the endpoint decides delivery; a missing header is the recipient's defect and never faults the mediator. | Over the TestServer, `mediate-request` with `return_route: all` returns the grant as the HTTP 200 body; the same request without the header returns 202 and the handler still produced the grant. |
| FR-MED-04 | MUST | **Threading (DD-11).** Every mediator response sets `thid` = the request's `thid` if present, else its `id` (FR-THR-01; CM 3.0 is silent; pickup 4.0 does this; harmless to peers that ignore it). `CoordinateMediation.ResponseThreadId` and `MessagePickup.ResponseThreadId` apply Core's rule: a declared `thid` that is not a valid thread id (empty included; only a hand-built message can carry one) is refused with `MalformedMessageException` quoting no value (since #141; before, an empty one fell back to the `id`). The recipient role correlates on `thid` (on `pthid` for a problem report, and on `pthid` only: it is REQUIRED, DIDComm v2.1 problems.md L83: "`pthid` - REQUIRED. The value is the `thid` of the thread in which the problem occurred.", so a problem report without `pthid` completes no request whatever its `thid` names — since #100, ADV3-1; before, its `thid` was used), after the answer passed the identity rule (authcrypted by the mediator to the requesting DID). A reply completes a request only when its `thid` (a problem report's `pthid`) names it (compared ignoring case, FR-MSG-04; since #141), on every path, the HTTP response to that request included; without `thid` it completes none and is logged (rate-limited, the answer type only): DIDComm v2.1 threading says all subsequent messages in a thread MUST include its `thid` and messages that do not share it MUST NOT be considered part of the same thread (threading.md L17), a message without `thid` is a thread of its own (its `id` MUST be treated as its `thid`, message_structure.md L130), and a message that continues a protocol MUST use a `thid` (problems.md L55). Received on its own (over WebSocket), a thread-less `delivery` is a live push (FR-PICK-08), never an answer; in an HTTP response it reaches nothing. (As built in #96 the client fell back to message type + mediator DID — the only waiting request of the pair that expected the type took a thread-less answer — which let a late thread-less answer to a request given up on complete a later request of the same channel; removed in #98, PR #130 review. Until the owner answered "2" after that review's second round, the HTTP response to a request was its answer with or without `thid`.) | Each response's `thid` equals the request's `thid` (else its `id`) in the fixtures; a reply without `thid` in the HTTP response to the request completes nothing and the request fails at once as unanswered, whatever its type (`mediate-grant`, `status`, `delivery`, a problem report without `pthid`), a `delivery` never reaching a live stream, while the same reply with the request's `thid` completes it, and a problem report whose `thid` but not `pthid` names the request completes nothing, through the correlator or in the HTTP response (`MediatorResponseMatcherTests`; a Warning naming only the answer type: `MediatorClientLogLevelTests`); against a 2.0 host that omits `thid` the `mediate-request` fails as unanswered (`MediatorClientCoordinateMediation20Tests`); over HTTP an acknowledgement's `status-request` is answered only by a status with its `thid` (`PickupClientAckThreadingTests`); unbound, a thread-less answer completes no request, with one candidate or several, and the same answer with its `thid` does (`MediatorResponseMatcherTests`, `MediatorClientChannelQa1Tests`); a late thread-less status answers neither the next chunk of an acknowledgement, its `status-request`, nor the next call on the same channel (`PickupClientAckThreadingTests`). |
| FR-MED-05 | MUST | **Peer identity and ownership policy (D5, DD-14).** The peer of every request is the authenticated sender DID (`DidSubjectOf(SenderKid)`); a `mediate-request`, `recipient-update` or `recipient-query` without an authcrypt layer (plaintext, anoncrypt, signed-only or `anoncrypt(sign(…))`; FR-RR-08's replay scope leaves these to this rule) is never granted or applied and is not answered — no session can exist for it (FR-RR-03) — and is logged with the id only (same rule as FR-PICK-01). One grant exists per peer DID. A `mediate-request` from a peer that already holds a grant is idempotent: the existing grant is re-issued with the same `routing_did` and registrations retained; the policy is asked about new grants only, so a peer that holds a grant is not re-evaluated (a host ends a grant with `IMediationStore.RevokeAsync`). `mediate-deny` is produced by the policy, or when the store cannot hold another grant (FR-MED-08). A `recipient_did` may be registered under one grant only: `add` of a DID already under the same grant → `no_change`; under another grant → `client_error`; `remove` of a DID not under the caller's grant → `client_error`; a store fault → `server_error` (caught and logged, never thrown through dispatch). The one-grant-per-DID rule is enforced inside the store as a single atomic check-and-insert, never in the policy: `IMediationPolicy.MayRegisterAsync` is an additional gate in front of it (default: allow), and a policy that refuses an `add` yields `client_error`, one that throws `server_error` for that entry. A peer that holds no grant gets `client_error` for every `recipient-update` entry and an empty page for `recipient-query`. The mediator's own DIDs — the DID that decrypted the request, the routing DIDs in the peer's grant, the routing DIDs configured now (which may be newer than the grant) and the host's `OwnIdentifiers` — cannot be registered as recipients (`client_error`); if that list cannot be read (the identity provider faults) the guard fails closed: every entry of the message is answered `server_error` and nothing is applied. `mediate-request` and `recipient-query` have no error shape in the protocol: a store, policy or identity fault while handling one grants nothing, produces no reply and is logged by exception type only. The policy is pluggable: `IMediationPolicy` decides grant/deny (default: grant) and may require stronger proof of DID ownership (the protocol page's endnote acknowledges squatting). `recipient_did` MUST be a bare DID (no path, query or fragment); a DID URL is answered `client_error`, never normalized. The registry is keyed by DID subject (§4.3), never by raw string; for the FR-ROUTE-11 lookup `next` is reduced to its DID subject. Because the spec fixes the `result` enum, an enrolled peer can learn whether a DID is registered under another grant (`client_error`); this is accepted — it is visible only to authenticated, enrolled peers and is removable by an `IMediationPolicy` that requires proof of ownership. | An anoncrypt, signed-only or `anoncrypt(sign)` `mediate-request` → no grant stored, no reply produced, a log entry; Mallory cannot `add` a DID Bob registered (`client_error`) nor `remove` it (`client_error`); Bob re-adding his own DID → `no_change`; a faulting store → `server_error` with the dispatcher unaffected; a faulting identity provider → every entry `server_error`, nothing applied, and the mediator's current routing DID is not registered; a routing DID configured after the grant was issued → `client_error`; a custom policy that denies is honored. |
| FR-MED-06 | MUST | **Pagination bounds.** `limit` is clamped to a host-configurable maximum page size (default 100); `limit` ≤ 0 yields an empty page; `offset` beyond the end yields an empty page with `remaining: 0`. `pagination.count` is the number returned, `offset` the offset applied, `remaining` the number left after the page. Without `paginate` the first page at the maximum size is returned and `pagination` is still present. Non-integer `limit` / `offset` are malformed: a string, or a number with a non-zero fractional part; JSON has one number type, so `10.0` and `1e1` read as 10. A negative `offset` is malformed. An integer beyond the 32-bit range, of any size, is clamped, never wrapped. | `limit: 0`, `offset > count`, `limit > max`, and the no-`paginate` case produce the stated pages; `limit: "10"`, `limit: 1.5` and `offset: -1` are rejected as malformed; `limit: 1e40` is clamped. |
| FR-MED-07 | MUST | **`routing_did` shape.** Each `routing_did` in `mediate-grant` MUST be a DID whose document has a `DIDCommMessaging` service whose `serviceEndpoint.uri` is a URI whose scheme the transport router handles (`http(s)` / `ws(s)`, FR-TRN-01; TLS in production per §9.2), never a DID, so senders resolve it per FR-ROUTE-04 (implicit `keyAgreement` prepend) with no recursive endpoint resolution ("A DID representing a mediator SHOULD NOT use alternative endpoints in its own DID Document"). The recipient's resulting service entry is `{uri: routing_did, accept: ["didcomm/v2"]}` (`DidCommServiceBuilder.ForMediator(routing_did)` builds it); no explicit `routingKeys` are needed for the one-mediator case. **Checked at start-up (as built).** `AddMediator()` requires at least one routing DID — in `MediatorOptions.CoordinateMediation.RoutingDids` or from a host `IMediatorIdentity` registered before it (`MediatorIdentity` is a ready-made fixed list); the first is the primary one. **List order is meaningful (owner decision 2026-11-12, #99):** the first routing DID is listed first in 3.0 grants, is the only one a 2.0 grant carries (FR-MED-10), and is the `from` of the mediator's out-of-band invitation. To move to a new DID, a host lists the new one first and keeps the old one configured, with its keys: forwards addressed to any listed routing DID keep being relayed, whichever one a grant named. A grant already issued keeps the DIDs it was issued with, so a repeated `mediate-request` gets the same answer. The order is not refused or checked; it is documented on `CoordinateMediationOptions.RoutingDids`, `IMediatorIdentity`, `MediatorIdentity` and `AddMediator()`. A registration where nothing could supply one is refused with `ArgumentException`. In a generic host, `MediatorHostedService` checks before the server listens (`IHostedLifecycleService.StartingAsync`), for each routing DID in order: it is a bare DID of at most 2048 characters; it resolves; the **first** `DIDCommMessaging` entry — the one senders use — has a `uri` that is an absolute `http`, `https`, `ws` or `wss` URI, not a DID and not the queue URI (FR-RR-06); and at least one of its `keyAgreement` keys has a private key the registered `ISecretsResolver` holds (otherwise no forward to it could be decrypted). A resolution failure, an unsupported DID method, a resolver or secrets resolver that throws, and a check that takes longer than 30 seconds fail start-up too: senders resolve the same DID, so the mediator would be unreachable. Routing keys on that first entry (the spec's SHOULD NOT; senders ignore them) and a later entry whose `uri` is a DID are logged as Warnings and do not fail start-up. Every failure is an `InvalidOperationException` whose message names the DID's index in the list (and, for a resolution failure, the exception type) and never the DID, an endpoint or exception text; a failed check fails the host's start. The same start-up also refuses (`InvalidOperationException`) forward settings in effect that are not the mediator's, whichever way the container was composed: a `ForwardProcessorOptions` handed out without the `MaxForwardDelay` cap (or with a longer one) or with another `RewrapMode` (one registered after `AddMediator()`), `RewrapMode.ReanoncryptToNext` together with an `IRecipientRoutingKeyProvider` other than the mediator's default (re-wrapping never reads it), and a forward recipient directory that is still the routing mediator's empty in-memory default (every forward would be dropped as unknown). Outside a generic host the hosted service does not run, so these checks do not run either (each role still checks its own settings when first used). | Start-up fails, naming the index and never the DID, for a routing DID whose first service `uri` is a DID; one with no `DIDCommMessaging` service or an empty first `uri`; a first `uri` that is `ftp:`, `mailto:`, the queue URI, relative or without a scheme; an unresolvable DID and an unsupported method; no `keyAgreement` key whose secret is held (a held authentication key alone is not enough); a DID resolver or secrets resolver that throws (by type, without its text); a resolver that hangs (after 30 s on an injected clock); a host `IMediatorIdentity` with no DID, with a DID URL, or that throws; and no DID resolution registered. `http`, `https`, `ws` and `wss` endpoints pass; only the first entry counts; routing keys on the mediator's own entry and a DID-valued later entry start with a Warning (`RoutingDidValidatorTests`, `MediatorHostedServiceTests`); a real generic host refuses to start with an unusable routing DID (`MediatorGenericHostTests`); `AddMediator()` with no routing DID and nothing that could supply one is refused at registration and adds nothing (`AddMediatorDiTests`); over the real endpoints, a receive limit too small for one pickup delivery, a service `uri` that is a DID, no service, an unsupported scheme and an unheld key-agreement secret each fail start-up without naming the DID, a sink registered after `AddMediator()` fails start-up, and `http` and `ws` services start and publish the invitation (`MediatorStartupTests`), each violation failing before the server answers (`MediatorStartupQaTests`); forward settings registered after `AddMediator()` with no cap, a longer cap or another mode, rewrap with a provider set through a host `Configure` or registered later, and no forward directory fail start-up, while a shorter cap with the same mode, pass-through with a later provider, and a host directory start (`AddMediatorQaTests`); Alice's `SendAsync(Forward: true)` to Bob (service `uri` = `routing_did`) builds exactly one forward layer addressed to the mediator's keys — Bob's attachment unpacks with his own keys directly (the FR-ROUTE-04 Example 1 assertion, `MediatorEndToEndTests`). |
| FR-MED-08 | MUST | **Identifier caps and store bounds (D7).** `recipient_did` and `routing_did` are wire DIDs under FR-API-09 (≤ 2048 UTF-16 code units, linear scan, rejected before any DID parse or resolver call, never echoed in error text, exceptions or logs; a protocol reply carries the value only where the protocol requires it (`updated[].recipient_did`, `dids[].recipient_did` in `recipient` and `keys[].recipient_did` in the 2.0 `keylist`, `status.recipient_did`, `delivery.recipient_did`) and only after the FR-API-09 cap check passed). The in-memory store bounds grants (`MaxGrants`, default 1 000), recipients per grant (`MaxRecipientsPerGrant`, default 256) and recipients in total (`MaxRecipients`, default 10 000), all host-configurable — the defaults are modest on purpose (owner decision of 2026-11-12: the in-memory stores are for samples, tests and small hosts; worst-case key memory at the defaults is about 45 MB, 11 000 keys of up to 4 KiB); exceeding the per-grant bound yields `client_error`, the total bound `server_error` (the mediator's limit, not the peer's), and the grant bound `mediate-deny`. One `recipient-update` carries at most `MaxUpdatesPerMessage` entries (default 1024); a longer list is malformed and is refused from its length alone — before the body is re-read and before any entry is decoded or allocated — and a peer without a grant costs one store read, no DID parse and no policy call. Grants do not expire, so under a policy that grants every authcrypt sender self-made DIDs can exhaust the grant and recipient bounds — new peers are then denied and new registrations fail while existing ones keep working; this is a stated limit of open enrolment (as W-2 is for the replay budget), closed by an allow-listing or rate-limiting `IMediationPolicy`. Grant lookup by `recipient_did` (used by FR-ROUTE-11) is constant-work with respect to registration state: one keyed probe for a hit and for a miss, taking no lock, with no second step that only a hit takes (a hit compares one key more than a miss, as any hash lookup does). No DID appears in log messages at Information or above. | cap + 1 `recipient_did` → `MalformedMessageException` without echo and without a resolver call; 10k adds → `client_error` once the bound is hit, memory bounded; 1025 entries with `MaxUpdatesPerMessage` 1024 → malformed, nothing applied; with a bound of 1, two entries whose second is itself malformed → the count is what is reported; registered-vs-unregistered lookup: medians of interleaved batches of same-length keys stay within a small constant factor (the gate allows 3×; about 1.1× measured), and both answers complete synchronously from one probe. |
| FR-MED-09 | MUST | **Recipient role (client).** Provide `MediationClient` (package and namespace `DidComm.Mediator.Client`; no ASP.NET Core): `RequestMediationAsync` (sends `mediate-request` with `ReturnRoute: All`; over HTTP the reply arrives in `SendResult.Returned`, over WebSocket via inbound dispatch; `mediate-deny` is a result, not an exception: the method returns `null`), `UpdateRecipientsAsync` (1 to 1024 updates in one message, the mediator's default limit; the answer must name each update's DID and action at its position), `QueryRecipientsAsync` (one page) and `QueryAllRecipientsAsync` (the page walker, which throws rather than stop early in silence), and `DidCommServiceBuilder.ForMediation(grant)` producing the FR-MED-07 service entry from the grant's first routing DID (plus `.WithRoutingKeys(...)` for a two-mediator route). **As built (#96):** `AddMediatorClient(configure?)` registers it and `PickupClient` over one internal channel, an inbound correlator; over WebSocket the host also registers `UseWebSocketTransport(o => o.ReceiveInbound = true)` and `UseInboundDispatch()`, and a WebSocket route whose answer could not reach the client is refused with `InvalidOperationException` before anything is sent; a client built without DI works over HTTP only. The route is the mediator's first `DIDCommMessaging` entry, selected as `SendAsync` selects a direct return-route endpoint (a mediator reached through routing is refused), or the caller's `serviceEndpointOverride`. Correlation uses a bounded pending map (`MaxPendingRequests`, 64 by default; a request beyond it is refused before sending) — identity first (an authcrypt whose `from` is its sender; its `to` is advisory, as DIDComm v2.1 message_structure says: a present `to` that does not name the decrypting DID is logged as a rate-limited Warning naming only the answer type, never a DID, and the answer is still matched, #98 owner decision C2), then the thread (FR-MED-04), then the request's pair: only an answer authcrypted by the request's mediator to the requesting DID completes it, and a stranger's own authcrypt naming a waiting thread is refused by that pair check exactly as a miss — with a timeout whose error names the request id, not the DID: `RequestTimeout`, 30 s by default, for each step (finding the endpoint, the Discover Features query, each request); over HTTP a response that does not carry the answer fails the request at once with that `TimeoutException`. Every request is safe to repeat. An in-thread problem report, or an answer that is malformed or does not match the request (more than 16 routing DIDs or one that is not a bare DID, a page of more than 1000 DIDs or with inconsistent `pagination`), is `MediationProtocolException` (the sanitized problem code on `ProblemCode`).
