The client negotiates the version through Discover Features first, preferring 3.0 and falling back to 2.0 (FR-MED-10), and remembers the outcome per mediator for an hour (at most 64 mediators). **The question and the role rule (owner decision 2026-11-18, #99):** the query message carries two queries, `https://didcomm.org/coordinate-mediation/3.*` and `…/2.*`, which the spec's wildcard, a whole-id regular expression (the Identus mediator) and a prefix with `.*` removed (the Blocktrust mediator) all read the same way; a disclosure counts unless its `roles` name `recipient` without `mediator` — absent roles, an empty list and a list of names the protocol does not define (Identus's `responder`) say nothing — and a `roles` value that is not an array does not count; `MediatorClientOptions.CoordinateMediationVersion` pins `V3` or `V2` for a mediator without Discover Features, and a query that is not answered fails with `TimeoutException`, never a guess. The Discover Features query is itself sent with `ReturnRoute: All`; every request, including that query, is timestamped per FR-RR-09; a mediator that discloses neither version fails the enrollment with `MediationNotSupportedException`, which carries the mediator's disclosed protocols, cleaned (at most 32), on `DisclosedProtocols` and quotes no wire value and no DID in its text. | Against the Phase 7 TestServer: enroll → DID document → Alice sends → delivered, over HTTP and over WebSocket (`MediatorClientEnrolmentTests`; over HTTP, a WebSocket named by the mediator's DID and an `http` DID with a `ws` override, `MediatorClientEnrolmentQa1Tests.Acceptance_flow_…`; through fetch to `status` 0, `PickupClientAcceptanceQa2Tests`); deny path returns a denied result (`null` for a policy deny, `MediatorClientEnrolmentTests.A_policy_deny_is_null_and_an_allowed_peer_is_granted`, and for a full grant store, `MediatorClientEnrolmentQa1Tests`); timeout → `TimeoutException` naming the request id (`MediatorClientEnrolmentTests.A_mediator_that_cannot_answer_is_an_immediate_TimeoutException_naming_the_request_and_no_DID`, `MediatorClientTimeoutRaceTests`, `MediatorClientChannelTests`); a 2.0-only fake mediator is driven through the 2.0 shapes over HTTP with `thid`, and without it its `mediate-request` fails as unanswered (`MediatorClientCoordinateMediation20Tests`). Also: a host disclosing neither version is `MediationNotSupportedException` with nothing else sent (`MediatorClientCoordinateMediation20Tests`); negotiation, the cache and the pin (`MediationClientNegotiationTests`, `NegotiatedVersionCacheTests`); arguments refused before sending, the grant, update and page checks, the walker (`MediationClientTests`, `MediationClientRecipientsTests`); a WebSocket route without inbound dispatch, with `ReceiveInbound` off or under STOMP is refused before anything is sent (`MediatorClientReceiveRouteTests`, `MediatorClientReceiveRouteInteropTests`, `MediatorClientDiTests`); the identity and threading rules of the matcher, against post-sign tampering and self-consistent forgeries (`MediatorResponseMatcherTests`), and forged answers arriving through the dispatcher and at the host's own receive endpoint, a stranger's probe of a waiting thread looking exactly like a miss (`MediatorClientChannelAdv1Tests`, `MediatorClientHostEndpointAdv1Tests`); `ForMediation` (`MediationServiceBuilderExtensionsTests`); a host that is mediator and recipient at once (`MediatorAndRecipientHostTests`). |
| FR-MED-10 | SHOULD | **Coordinate Mediation 2.0 compatibility.** (SHOULD for the library; required for the Phase 7 interop exit criterion, since the available external mediators speak only 2.0.) Also handle `https://didcomm.org/coordinate-mediation/2.0` (what the Identus and Blocktrust mediators and their wallets speak): `mediate-grant.body.routing_did` is a **string**; `keylist-update` / `keylist-update-response` / `keylist-query` / `keylist` replace `recipient-update` / `recipient-update-response` / `recipient-query` / `recipient`; `keylist.body.keys` (not `dids`); the inner fields (`recipient_did`, `action`, `result`) and their values are identical; requests carry `return_route: all`. 2.0 and 3.0 share one registry (a DID registered through either is the same tenant) and both PIURIs are disclosed. A `keylist` page is clamped like a `recipient` page and always carries `pagination`. Replies always use the canonical type strings. A request without `return_route` is still applied and answered; the endpoint decides how the answer travels. `AddCoordinateMediation()` (and so `AddMediator()`) registers it next to the 3.0 handler unless `CoordinateMediationOptions.EnableV2` (default `true`) is `false`. `EnableV2` is read when that call runs, because it decides what is registered; a value in effect that disagrees with what was registered (`Configure<CoordinateMediationOptions>`, configuration binding, two calls that disagree) fails with `InvalidOperationException` naming the property, never a DID, when the handlers are first built (at start-up under `AddMediator()` in a generic host). 2.0 is a mediator-service PIURI, so its requests are in the FR-RR-08 replay scope and stamped by FR-RR-09. The 2.0 message codec (`CoordinateMediation20`, `DidComm.Mediator.Client`) is internal and now works in both directions; 2.0 adds no public message API. **Recipient side, as built (#96, #99):** `MediationClient` (FR-MED-09) speaks 2.0 — chosen through Discover Features when a mediator discloses no 3.0 (since #99 the question and the role rule also work with the answers of the 2.0 mediators in the field, owner decision 2026-11-18; FR-MED-09), or pinned with `MediatorClientOptions.CoordinateMediationVersion = V2` (the issue's "preferred version") — through the same internal codec, which writes the three requests and reads the four answers in the shapes above (a `routing_did` array is accepted too). **Interop notes (source of the real implementations, #99):** both 2.0 mediators emit a string `routing_did`, ignore `paginate` and never send `pagination`; ### 8c. Message Pickup (FR-PICK-*)

(Source: https://didcomm.org/messagepickup/3.0/, status Production; PIURI `https://didcomm.org/messagepickup/3.0`; roles `mediator`, `recipient`; "the `return_route` header extension must be set to `all` in all request submitted by the `recipient`". Package: `DidComm.Mediator` for the mediator role (D1); the recipient role and the builders and parsers both roles use are in `DidComm.Mediator.Client` (#113). 3.0 does not define how a live-delivered message is framed; DD-13 records the choice. Message Pickup 4.0 (`https://didcomm.org/message-pickup/4.0`, note the renamed PIURI; source `decentralized-identity/didcomm.org@a26926c`, `site/content/protocols/messagepickup/4.0/readme.md`) is built beside it in FR-PICK-12 (#100): one mailbox, either version.)

| ID | Keyword | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| FR-PICK-01 | MUST | **Protocol identity and advertisement.** Implement `https://didcomm.org/messagepickup/3.0` with roles `mediator` and `recipient`; the mediator role (`DidComm.Mediator.Pickup`, registered with `AddMessagePickup()`) is disclosed with `roles: ["mediator"]` only when registered. Every recipient request carries `return_route: all` (every HTTP POST / once per WebSocket); a request without an authcrypt layer (plaintext, anoncrypt, signed-only or `anoncrypt(sign(…))`; FR-RR-08's replay scope leaves these to this rule) is neither applied nor answered (logged, id only, rate-limited) and its type and body are never read. The peer is the DID subject of the authcrypt sender key (the rule that opens a return-route session, FR-RR-03), never the plaintext `from`. Messages of the protocol that the mediator role does not act on (`status`, `delivery`, an unknown name) are ignored. The role needs the mediation registry (`IMediationStore`, FR-MED-05): `AddMessagePickup()` may be called before or after `AddCoordinateMediation()`, and without a registry the first use fails with a message naming the fix. Replies and deliveries travel on the request's return-route session, so over a WebSocket the endpoint's same-socket replies must be enabled (FR-RR-05; `MapDidCommMediator` does it, #95). **4.0 (#100):** `AddMessagePickup()` (and so `AddMediator()`) also registers `MessagePickup40Handler`, and Discover Features discloses `https://didcomm.org/message-pickup/4.0` with `roles: ["mediator"]` too, unless `MessagePickupOptions.EnableV4` (default `true`) is `false`; the same peer, store and session rules apply to both versions (FR-PICK-12). | Disclosure present only with the pickup mediator role registered; an anoncrypt, signed-only or `anoncrypt(sign)` `status-request` → no reply and a log entry, also when its body is malformed; such a `messages-received` removes nothing and such a `live-delivery-change` changes nothing; a plaintext `from` naming another peer does not change whose queue is served; registration without a mediation registry fails at first use with `InvalidOperationException`. Both pickup PIURIs disclosed by default, only 3.0 with `EnableV4 = false`, only 4.0 for a host that registers `MessagePickup40Handler` by hand (`MessagePickup40DiTests`). |
| FR-PICK-02 | MUST | **`status-request` → `status`.** `status-request` body: `recipient_did` (optional; when present the mediator MUST report only that DID's queue). `status` body: `message_count` (REQUIRED) and, optionally, `recipient_did` (MUST echo the request's value when it had one), `longest_waited_seconds`, `newest_received_time` and `oldest_received_time` (UTC epoch **seconds** as integers), `total_bytes`, `live_delivery`. This implementation always writes `message_count`, `total_bytes` and `live_delivery` (the flag of the session the request arrived on, FR-PICK-06), writes `recipient_did` exactly when the request had one, and writes the three time members when the count is above zero. The count covers the messages held under the peer's grant that are due and not expired, **including delivered-but-unacknowledged ones** (FR-PICK-04: the count drops on acknowledgement); a message whose not-before time has not passed is retained and counts against the quota but is not reported until due. **Whose queue:** a peer sees only what is held under its own mediation grant. A `recipient_did` is honoured only when it is registered under the peer's grant (`IMediationStore.FindGrantByRecipientDidAsync`, one lookup, the grant's peer then compared with the authenticated peer; "registered under another grant" and "registered by nobody" take that same single lookup and nothing else); a DID registered under another grant, registered by nobody, or not a bare DID gets the answer of an empty queue — `status` echoing the value with `message_count: 0` — so the reply says nothing about other recipients (a smaller surface than FR-MED-05's accepted `client_error`), and the message store is not consulted; a peer without a grant gets `message_count: 0`. A `recipient_did` over 2048 characters is malformed (FR-API-09). `status` carries `thid` = the request's thread (its `thid` if present, else its `id`; DD-11 — the 3.0 page shows no `thid` on `status`, 4.0 requires it, and the recipient client correlates on it). A `status` message MUST NOT be put on the queue and MUST only be sent while the recipient is actively connected (an HTTP request awaiting its response, or a WebSocket session): the handler only ever returns it as the reply to the request it answers — it is never stored and never sent out of band — and the endpoints send a reply only on the request's return-route session (FR-RR-04/05). | Fixture round-trip for a `status` with only `message_count`; `recipient_did` filter scoping (one grant cannot see another grant's queue; the reply and the store calls for another grant's DID equal those for an unregistered DID); times are integer seconds and `longest_waited_seconds` follows the injected clock; a delivered-unacknowledged message is still counted, a not-yet-due one is not; `thid` equals the request's thread; a `status` produced with no session is never stored in the queue and is not sent by the endpoints. |
| FR-PICK-03 | MUST | **`delivery-request` → `delivery` or `status`.** `delivery-request` body: `limit` (REQUIRED non-negative integer; the mediator SHOULD deliver up to it) and `recipient_did` (optional filter, authorised as in FR-PICK-02). The mediator clamps `limit` to a host-configurable batch maximum (`MaxDeliveryBatch`, default 10) before the store is touched, and rejects a non-integer or negative `limit` as malformed without any work that depends on its value (an integer of any size is clamped; `10.0` reads as 10); `limit: 0` delivers nothing and is answered with `status` (not malformed). `delivery` carries `thid` = the request's thread (`thid` if present, else `id`; DD-11 — for every request of the protocol page that is the request `id`; a request whose thread id exceeds 512 characters is malformed, which bounds the echo; unpack already refuses one, FR-MSG-11, so this check is what a direct caller of the dispatcher meets), `body.recipient_did` only when the request had one, and `attachments: [{id, data: {base64}}]` holding the encrypted DIDComm messages exactly as received — "The ONLY valid type of attachment … is a DIDComm v2 Message in encrypted form" — relayed as bytes (no inner validation). Attachment data is written as base64url without padding (DIDComm v2.1 attachments) and read leniently (padding and the standard alphabet accepted; `data.json` accepted). The handler pushes the `delivery` through the request's return-route session and returns no further reply; over HTTP that pushed message is the single 200 response body, carrying up to `limit` attachments. A request dispatched without a session leases nothing and is answered `status` (nothing could carry the batch, and a lease would only lock the queue). The batch is bounded by **count and bytes**. *Estimate:* the mediator selects the longest FIFO prefix of deliverable messages, up to the clamped `limit`, whose payload total does not exceed `BatchPayloadBudget` — the payload bytes that fit a packed `delivery` of at most `max_delivery_bytes` (`MaxDeliveryBytes`; default `DidCommOptions.MaxReceiveBytes`, 1 MiB; hosts set it to what their recipients accept, since a recipient discards an over-cap response, FR-RR-07) after setting aside fixed allowances: 32 KiB for the JWE envelope, 8 KiB for the `delivery` without its attachments, 96 bytes per attachment; a payload of `n` bytes costs `⌈4n/3⌉` characters in the attachment and that text is expanded by 4/3 again as the envelope's base64url ciphertext, so the packed size is about 16/9 of the payload. At the defaults the budget is **564 525 bytes** (≈ 551 KiB). *Guarantee:* the packed size is then verified where the message is packed — the session's size-bounded send (FR-RR-04/05) measures the bytes handed to the transport and refuses a message over `max_delivery_bytes`; the handler gives the last attachment back to the queue and sends again until it fits. A message that is over the budget even alone in the `delivery` the request asked for (possible only for a peer whose own DID document inflates its envelope past the allowance, or for a record admitted under an earlier, larger budget) is neither delivered nor removed: it is **passed over** (logged, rate-limited) and the same request goes on to what is queued behind it — it leases again and delivers that — so a `status` answer to a `delivery-request` still means that nothing is deliverable now. Passing over is a mark the store keeps on the message itself (`IMessageStore.PassOverAsync`; `QueuedMessage.PassOvers` counts them): the message gives up the request's lease and is left out of every lease until an instant `LeaseTimeout × 2^n` ahead, `n` being the number of times it was passed over before — one lease timeout the first time, twice as long each time it is tried again and still does not fit. The mark has no owner and is ended by time only: not by lease expiry, not by a lease being given back, not by the connection closing. One request does a bounded amount of work whatever the queue holds — at most 2 × `MaxDeliveryBatch` lease rounds and as many packs; a request that runs out answers `status` and the next one continues — and that bound never turns into starvation: because what a request learned about such messages outlives the request, its lease and its connection, and the periods outgrow any polling interval, a recipient whose queue holds more such messages than one request can try, and who asks only at or beyond `LeaseTimeout` or on a new connection each time, still gets to what is deliverable behind them after a bounded number of requests. Nothing is deleted on a size measurement: the measured size includes the request's own thread id and filter, so another request, a larger budget or a smaller envelope may still deliver the message; it stays retained (counted in `status` and in the quota) until it expires, reaches the mailbox age limit (FR-PICK-10), or the host purges it. So that every retained payload is deliverable for a peer whose envelope is within the allowance, a payload larger than `max_admissible_payload` = min(`MaxReceiveBytes`, `BatchPayloadBudget`) is **not admitted** at enqueue (not retained, logged with its size only; the transport result stays 202 — FR-PICK-10); the same number bounds admission and every batch, so an admitted payload always fits alone whatever `limit` is (hosts that must relay `MaxReceiveBytes`-sized payloads raise `max_delivery_bytes` and their recipients' `MaxReceiveBytes` together). The number is logged when the role is first used and exposed as `MessagePickupQueue.MaxAdmissiblePayloadBytes`; a `max_delivery_bytes` that cannot carry any payload fails `MessagePickupOptions.Validate()` at registration (an unsatisfiable derived default fails when the role is first used). A count-limited batch therefore never produces a response the recipient cannot accept, and pickup 3.0's "deliver up to the limit" is satisfied with fewer items. If no message is available a `status` MUST be sent immediately instead. Delivery is FIFO per recipient; a message whose not-before time (FR-ROUTE-10) has not passed is skipped and one whose expiry has passed (FR-ROUTE-09) is never delivered. **Registration is checked when messages are delivered** (owner decision of 2026-11-12): a filtered request is authorised before the store is touched; in an unfiltered batch every distinct recipient DID is checked again, and the leased messages of a DID that no longer belongs to the peer's grant are not delivered and are removed — exactly those records, by id, so a message that arrives for the DID a moment later is judged by its own check — and the request goes on with the rest (if the check cannot be made, or the registry's answer names no usable peer, nothing is delivered or removed and the leases are released). **Only encrypted messages (#100, owner decision 2026-11-20).** Both versions allow only encrypted messages in a `delivery` (3.0 L172: "The ONLY valid type of attachment for this message is a DIDComm v2 Message in encrypted form."; 4.0 L261: "…a DIDComm v1 or v2 Message in encrypted form."), while routing only asks a sender to encrypt what it forwards (v2.1 routing.md L57: "This content should be encrypted for the next recipient."). So the mailbox refuses any message that is not encrypted, whoever supplies it, and stores nothing: `MessagePickupQueue.DeliverAsync` answers `PickupDeliveryOutcome.NotEncrypted` (the forward sink counts it as `Dropped-PayloadNotEncrypted`; the forward's sender already has its 202). *Encrypted form* (our definition, from v2.1 message_structure.md L65/L71): the outermost layer is a JWE — anoncrypt or authcrypt — judged without decrypting: valid UTF-8 throughout (checked first: the JSON reader does not validate the bytes inside strings and member names; #100, ADV2-1) and strict JSON (no duplicate member names, nothing after the value) holding an object with a `ciphertext` member, the rule the library's unpack uses to classify a JWE (FR-API-03). Plaintext, a signed-only message (JWS) and anything that is not a JSON object are refused. In rewrap mode, and in pass-through mode with an extra routing key, what is stored is the mediator's own JWE, so a non-encrypted inner message passes there (the recipient's client opens it). | HTTP `delivery-request{limit: 3}` with 5 queued → 200 with one `delivery` carrying 3 attachments and `thid` = request id; empty queue → `status`; `limit: 2^31-1` and `1e400` clamped (the store is asked for the batch maximum), `limit: 0` → `status`, `limit: -1` and `limit: "10"` rejected; a delayed message is skipped until its not-before time; five 300 KiB payloads queued under a 1 MiB `max_delivery_bytes`, `limit: 10` → each `delivery` carries exactly 1 attachment, the recipient acks after each, five deliveries then `status` — over HTTP and over a WebSocket session; every packed response is asserted ≤ `max_delivery_bytes`; a payload of exactly `max_admissible_payload` is admitted and its single-attachment `delivery` packs within the budget, one byte more is refused at enqueue; with the 1 MiB default a 600 KiB payload is refused at enqueue while a 500 KiB payload is admitted; a recipient with many key-agreement keys (envelope larger than the allowance) gets a trimmed batch, every response within the budget; a message that does not fit alone stays stored and the one queued behind it is delivered by the same request, also for a recipient that polls less often than the lease lasts; with a batch maximum of 1, two messages that do not fit and a deliverable third, polls 61 s apart (lease 60 s) deliver the third by the third poll; more such messages than one request can try (several times the 2 × batch budget), polls at 60 s, 61 s and far beyond, and a recipient that reconnects for every request without time passing: the deliverable messages behind them arrive in a bounded number of requests, each request within its work bound, nothing removed; the period a message is passed over for doubles each time and a closed connection does not end it; the same message is delivered later by a request whose framing lets it fit; one request never packs more than 2 × the batch maximum; a `max_delivery_bytes` below the fixed allowances fails registration; a DID removed from the grant: its leased messages are not delivered and are removed by id, the rest of the batch is delivered, and a message accepted for that DID after it was registered again is not removed by a request that saw it gone a moment earlier; a request without a session leases nothing. A plaintext, General-JWS or Flattened-JWS forward gets its 202 and is dropped (`Dropped-PayloadNotEncrypted`), and the recipient's 3.0 `status` counts 0 and its 4.0 `delivery` is empty; a host's `DeliverAsync` with plaintext, a JWS, non-JSON, a JSON array or duplicate member names → `NotEncrypted`, nothing stored, no quota used, no live push in either version; anoncrypt and authcrypt are stored; rewrap and extra-routing-key paths store the added JWE (`MessagePickupNotEncryptedTests`, `MediatorForwardSinkTests`, `MediatorNotEncryptedTests`). |
| FR-PICK-04 | MUST | **Attachment ids and acknowledgement.** Each attachment `id` is a mediator-generated opaque value, unique to the mediator and scoped to the grant — never the inner message id, which the mediator cannot read. Ids are minted by the queue itself from the operating system's CSPRNG — 16 random bytes as 32 lowercase hex characters (unreserved URI characters, within the 64-character bound on minted ids) — and never through `IMessageIdGenerator`, so a host's sequential or guessable generator cannot make one recipient's ids predictable from another's (owner decision of 2026-11-12); the store keys every record by (grant, id), so an id means something only under the grant it was issued for. Delivered messages MUST NOT be deleted until acknowledged by `messages-received` `{"message_id_list": [id, …]}`; delivered-but-unacknowledged messages hold a lease (not re-delivered within it; `LeaseTimeout`, default 60 s) and become deliverable again — the same record, same id, same position, never a copy — when the lease expires or, for a WebSocket session, when that session closes (the session's `Closed` signal, FR-RR-05); HTTP leases expire by timeout only; under `MapDidCommMediator` an HTTP delivery that the client has not taken within the lease is aborted at the lease, so a client holds at most one copy of a leased record (#98). Ending the leases of a closed session is background work with bounds of its own: one store call at a time, from a backlog of at most 1 024 closed sessions; a session that never took a lease queues nothing; when the backlog is full the close is not queued and those leases end by their timeout; closing a session never waits for the store. A lease has an identity: a request that gives messages back (a trimmed batch, a push the session did not take) ends only its own lease, never one a later request took on the same message. Delivery is therefore at-least-once: a recipient may see a message again after a lease ran out or a connection closed mid-delivery, and acknowledges by id. On `messages-received` the mediator removes the listed messages of the peer's own grant whatever their lease state (unknown or foreign ids are ignored, never reported and never echoed; no grant lookup is needed, the operation is confined to the caller's own key) and SHOULD reply with an updated `status` — this implementation always does under 3.0. | A second `delivery-request` within the lease does not re-deliver (lease timeout injected via the clock); after lease release it does, with the same id; an ack removes the messages and `status.message_count` drops; an ack with unknown ids is ignored and `status` is still sent; a recipient cannot ack ids delivered to another grant (the same id string under two grants is two records); a late clean-up of one request never ends the lease another request took; a peer without a grant that turns live mode on and disconnects 1 000 times against a store whose release call does not complete leaves no clean-up call behind; connections that each leave a lease behind are cleaned up one call at a time with at most 1 024 waiting, the overflow's leases ending by timeout; with a sequential `IMessageIdGenerator` registered in the container, 1 000 queued messages get 1 000 distinct 32-character lowercase hex ids, the generator is never called, and the delivered attachment ids are those ids (`PickupRandomIdTests`). |
| FR-PICK-05 | MUST | **Multiple recipients.** A message that arrives addressed to several registered recipients (reachable through the host-facing enqueue API, `MessagePickupQueue.DeliverAsync`, called once per recipient; a routing/2.0 `forward` names a single `next`) MUST be queued for each recipient independently; one recipient's acknowledgement MUST NOT remove the other recipients' copies. | An enqueue for N recipients yields N independent records with distinct ids, each acknowledged separately. |
| FR-PICK-06 | MUST | **Live mode rules.** Live mode is disabled by default and only activated by the recipient (`live-delivery-change`); it MUST only be enabled on a persistent transport (a WebSocket session, FR-RR-05); messages that arrive while it is off MUST be queued; the live flag is per session, and a new inbound connection starts with live mode disabled (reset when the session's `Closed` signal fires, FR-RR-05 — not on the registry's lifecycle notifications, which are best-effort); messages already in the queue are not affected — live mode pushes only what enters the store after the `status` that answers the `live-delivery-change`, decided by the store's arrival sequence (the mark is that status's `QueueStatus.HighWaterSequence`, read in the same store step as its counts), never by clock readings, so a message is either counted in that `status` or pushed, whatever the clocks do; turned on again while on, the session keeps its first mark — and must still be requested with `delivery-request`. **A live push returned unconfirmed is never pushed live again** (owner decision 2026-11-20 on #100, "Never push it live again": "A message pushed live and returned unconfirmed waits until the recipient asks for it. Same rule for 3.0 and 4.0, since one mailbox serves both."; 4.0 L304 "Messages already in the queue are not affected by _Live Mode_; they **MUST** still be requested with `delivery-request` messages.", L308 "If a message is not acknowledged, the message **MUST** be added to the queue for later pickup."; 3.0 L203): when a live push's lease ends without `messages-received` — its session closed or the lease ran out — the message is back in the queue and from then on only a `delivery-request` delivers it, in either version. As built (ours; rebuilt in PR #143 review round 1 as an invariant): the push's lease says it is one (`MessageLease.ForPush`); the store marks a record in the same step as a push lease takes it (`QueuedMessage.PushTaken`, kept as long as the record is stored) and remembers which lease set the mark; a push lease request never takes a marked record; the mark is cleared only by `IMessageStore.ReturnUnsentAsync` or a pass-over by the lease that set it (also after that lease ended), which the coordinator calls only when the session answered `NotSent` or `TooLarge` (open or closing) or the send was never attempted (owner decision 2026-11-21, OD-5); `ReleaseAsync`, `ReleaseLeasesAsync`, expiry, faults and cancellations never clear it, in any order; the coordinator refuses a marked record a store hands to a live push (not pushed, still picked up); counts, quotas and `status` are unchanged. When a peer has several live sessions with the flag on, a new message is pushed to exactly one — the newest — because a lease has one owner. Turning the flag on or off needs no grant: it is per-session state bounded by the session registry, and pushes only ever follow a grant. | `live-delivery-change{true}` over WS → `status{live_delivery: true}`; socket close → the next connection's `status{live_delivery: false}`; queued messages remain queued when live mode turns on; a session closed without being unregistered receives no push and its leases are released; two live sessions → one push. Under `AddMediator()`, on a frozen clock what was stored before `live-delivery-change{true}` is picked up and what arrived after it is pushed, a message stored after the live-on `status` was read but before the session was live is pushed by the wake-up that turning live delivery on raises, and turning it on with nothing after the mark records no push outcome (`PushArrivalOrderTests`); in 3.0 and 4.0, a push left unacknowledged until its lease runs out with the connection open is not pushed again when a newer message arrives, the newer one is pushed, a `delivery-request` delivers the returned one and its acknowledgement removes it, a close mid-lease returns it to pickup with no push to an older or a reconnected live session, a push the session did not take is pushed at a later wake-up, and a grant at its quota stays at its quota (`PickupLiveNoRepushTests`); the mark is set on a close and on a push lease's expiry, never on a give-back by lease id, a pass-over or a `delivery-request`'s lease, survives a `delivery-request` lease that ends, changes no count, and a caller-set mark is ignored (`MessagePickupStoreLivePushReturnTests`); which leases are push leases, a push taken by a session that closed meanwhile is given back by id and stays marked, a push answered `NotSent` (open or closing) or never attempted is given back as unsent and stays eligible, a send that threw after it was attempted stays marked (OD-5; `MessagePickupLivePushExitMatrixTests`, 2 paths × 11 exits × 2 clean-up orders, and the reviewer's reproduction), and a marked record handed to a live push is refused (`MessagePickupLivePushReturnTests`; #100); after a backward clock step, and when a delivery read the clock before live delivery went on but stored its message after, the message is still pushed live (`PushDispatcherAdv5Tests`, ADV-5 defects 1 and 2); | FR-PICK-08 | MUST | **Live-mode framing under 3.0 (DD-13).** A live-delivered message is pushed as a `delivery` message with exactly one attachment and no `thid` (there is no `delivery-request`), through the session's size-bounded send, and the recipient is expected to acknowledge it with `messages-received`; a live delivery is the store's own copy under a lease owned by the session (FR-PICK-04) — under `AddMediator()` the message is stored first and a push worker then takes the oldest record that entered the store after the `status` that answered the session's `live-delivery-change`, under the session's lease (`IMessageStore.LeaseAsync` with `MessageLeaseRequest.AfterSequence` set to that status's `QueueStatus.HighWaterSequence`, FR-PICK-06/09), while a host that calls `MessagePickupQueue.DeliverAsync` with `AllowLive: true` (the default) gets the record admitted already leased to the session, in one store step — so an unacknowledged one simply has its lease released on lease expiry or disconnect — after which only a `delivery-request` delivers it, never another live push (FR-PICK-06; owner decision 2026-11-20 on #100): no second copy is ever made and the FR-PICK-10 budget is never exceeded by live mode. A push the session does not take (no free push slot, a downgraded or closing session, and — for a host's `DeliverAsync` with `AllowLive: true` — an envelope over the budget) leaves the message queued with its lease released at once; it is not retried on another session (under `AddMediator()` the push worker's operation stops there, and the message is tried again at the grant's next wake-up, FR-PICK-09). Under `AddMediator()` a message whose envelope is over the budget even alone is passed over, as a pickup does (FR-PICK-03), and the next one is pushed. A message that is not yet due (FR-ROUTE-10) is never pushed before it is due: `DeliverAsync` with `AllowLive: true` queues it for pickup, and under `AddMediator()` it goes, once due, with the grant's next push, or is picked up. No scheduler pushes it when it falls due, now or later (owner decision of 2026-11-14 on #88): a recipient in live mode polls — on connect and then periodically — and that polling is the permanent design, not a stopgap. `PickupClient.ReceiveLiveAsync` (FR-PICK-11) drains when it starts and one `LivePollInterval` (1 minute by default) after each drain ends, so keep the interval at or below the longest delay senders ask for (`MaxForwardDelay`, 1 hour by default). This is the Pickup 4.0 rule applied under 3.0, where the page is silent; note the consequence for a 3.0 recipient that never acknowledges live deliveries: they stay retained and counted until its quota is full. | With live mode on, an enqueue for the recipient arrives as a `delivery` push with one attachment; a refused push is deliverable at once; under `AddMediator()` the push worker takes what entered the store after the live-on `status`, oldest first, under the session's own lease, which the session's close gives back, a push the session does not take gives it back at once and ends the operation, and a message too large for the session's envelope is passed over while the next one is pushed (`LivePushTests`); concurrent enqueues across grants number every stored record once, without gaps and in each grant's arrival order, even with refusals interleaved, every status read while records are stored counts exactly the records at or below its own mark, the numbers keep rising across a store-full refusal, the store-wide expiry sweep, a grant purge and the expiry purge, and concurrent takes after a mark never return a record at or below it and take every later one exactly once (`MessagePickupStoreSequenceAdv6Tests`);
