| FR-PICK-09 | MUST | **Delivery order (D6).** For a routed payload the mediator tries, in order: (1) a live return-route session for the recipient's grant — the payload is first admitted to the store (FR-PICK-10 budget), then the stored record is pushed under a lease owned by that session (FR-PICK-08), (2) the pickup queue, (3) **an optional direct push to the recipient's own endpoint** (#110; `MediatorOptions.DirectPush`, off by default; split out of #95 by [owner decision](https://github.com/moisesja/didcomm-dotnet/issues/88#issuecomment-6871566704)): when the recipient has no live session, the stored message is posted, unchanged, to the endpoint the host registered for the recipient's grant in its `IRecipientEndpointDirectory` — the spec's mediator process ("Retrieve service endpoint pre-configured by recipient", routing.md L96–98), whose coordination with the recipient is out of the spec's scope (routing.md L94) — and never to an address a message carries; what is not pushed waits in the queue (the direct push below). A host that keeps the routing role only can still push forwards itself through its own `IForwardSink` (the #90 seam, with `AddRoutingMediator()`: `AddMediator()` relays every forward through its own sink and refuses a host's); `samples/04-MediatorAgent` shows both: a host relay (a host `IForwardSink` behind `AddRoutingMediator()` that sends with `DidCommClient.SendPackedAsync` to the endpoint it knows) in sections 1–8, and `AddMediator()` with `DirectPush` in section 9. Pushes and queue placement respect the not-before time (FR-ROUTE-10). Storing is `MessagePickupQueue.DeliverAsync(recipientDid, payload, options)`: it looks up the grant the recipient DID is registered under at that moment (no grant → not retained), stores the message under that grant, then **looks the registration up again** — when the DID no longer belongs to that grant (the grant was revoked, or the DID moved, while the message was being stored; FR-PICK-10) it removes exactly the record it stored and reports `NotRegistered` (if that removal fails, the record stays, under no lease, and is reported `Queued`), and when that second lookup fails it keeps the record, reports `Queued` and pushes nothing live — and reports `PushedLive`, `Queued`, `NotRegistered`, `Expired`, `PayloadTooLarge`, `QueueFull` or `Faulted`, never to the message's sender and never by throwing — only a cancellation before anything is stored throws; once the message is stored, a cancellation or a fault is reported `Queued` (the message stays, under no lease), and a delivery already in flight, a live push included, completes even if a concurrent acknowledgement or `RevokeAndPurgeAsync` removes the record. With `PickupDeliveryOptions.AllowLive` `true` (the default, for a host that calls it) the call also makes step (1) itself, on the caller's thread: the record is admitted already leased to the newest live session in one store step and pushed (`PushedLive` when the session takes it), so the call can take as long as packing the `delivery` for the recipient — which resolves the recipient's DID — takes; with `false` it only stores. `MessagePickupQueue.RemoveAsync` lets a host remove a retained message. **The pipeline (as built, `AddMediator()`).** The mediator's forward sink runs on `ForwardHandler`'s pump after the 202 and only stores: it calls `DeliverAsync(DidSubjectOf(next), payload, (NotBefore, ExpiresTime, AllowLive: false))` and never awaits a live session, because packing a `delivery` resolves the recipient's DID and the recipient controls how long that takes — so one recipient that is slow to take a push never holds up the storing of other recipients' messages. Every outcome other than `Queued` is a logged, rate-limited drop; each forward is counted once on `didcomm.forward.outcomes` — `Queued` for every stored forward, whatever happens to its push afterwards, otherwise `Dropped-Unknown`, `Dropped-Expired`, `Dropped-TooLarge`, `Dropped-Quota` or `Faulted` — and the transport answer stays 202. **Push workers** (`PushDispatcher`, running whenever `AddMediator()` is used). **The store decides what is pushed:** the workers keep no per-message state. After storing a message that is already due, the sink wakes the recipient's grant when the grant has, at that moment, a live session with live delivery on, or always when direct push is on; otherwise nothing is woken and the message waits for pickup. A wake-up is O(1), never blocks the pump and carries nothing of the message; wake-ups coalesce into one flag per grant, and one that arrives while the grant's operation runs is served right after it. An operation takes, one at a time, the next record the store hands out at that moment — due, unexpired, unleased, not passed over, oldest first (`IMessageStore.LeaseAsync` for one record) — and checks its registration at delivery time exactly as a pickup does (a record whose recipient DID no longer belongs to the grant is removed by id → `Push-Skipped-Unregistered`, and the next record is taken), so an expired, aged-out, acknowledged, purged or picked-up message is never pushed and never takes another message's place. The **live push** (`PickupCoordinator.PushNextLiveAsync`): the newest live session of the grant's peer with live delivery on, and only records that entered the store after the `status` that answered its `live-delivery-change` (`MessageLeaseRequest.AfterSequence` = that status's `QueueStatus.HighWaterSequence`: the store's arrival sequence, never a clock reading, so a message is either counted in that `status` — the recipient's to pick up, FR-PICK-06 — or pushed, whatever the clocks do; turning live delivery on also wakes the grant, so a message stored after that `status` was read but before the session was live, whose own wake-up found no live session, is pushed too), each under a lease owned by the session (for `LeaseTimeout`) and sent as a one-message `delivery` in the session's framing through the size-bounded send (`max_delivery_bytes`) — taken, it stays leased to the session until `messages-received`, the session's close or the lease timeout (`Pushed-Live`), and the next record is taken; too large for the session's envelope even alone, it is passed over as a pickup does (`Push-Failed`), and the next record is taken; not taken (no free push slot, a downgraded or closing session), a fault or a cancellation gives the lease back once the send has ended (`Push-Failed`) and ends the operation; when the peer has no such session, or nothing after its mark is left, the operation ends and the messages wait for pickup (`Push-Skipped-NoLiveSession` when the peer had no live session at the operation's first step). A wake-up that finds nothing left to push is no outcome. **The store's arrival sequence** (part of the `IMessageStore` contract): the store gives every record it stores a `QueuedMessage.Sequence` greater than that of every record it stored before (a value the caller set is ignored; one counter for the whole store will do, and `InMemoryMessageStore` keeps one), reports the highest one assigned in every `QueueStatus` (`HighWaterSequence`, read in the same step as the counts) and honours `MessageLeaseRequest.AfterSequence` (only records with a greater sequence); a host store must do the same. The push workers decide "arrived after" by it — after the live-on `status` — never by comparing two clock readings, and refuse a record handed out without a sequence or not after the one asked for (it is given back and not pushed, `Push-Failed`; pickup is unaffected); a status with a negative mark is outside the contract like a negative count. **Retries:** a live push the session did not take (it answered `NotSent` or `TooLarge`: no free push slot, a downgraded or closing session, a failed pack; a too-large one is passed over first) or that was never attempted is tried again at the grant's next wake-up (it never reached the recipient). A push whose send was attempted and then faulted or reached its deadline stays marked and is delivered only through pickup, like one the session took and that came back unacknowledged (owner decision 2026-11-21, OD-5: "only a connection's explicit 'not sent', or no attempt at all, keeps a message eligible for a live push"; FR-PICK-06); an operation makes at most one failed live attempt, a wake-up is a stored message or a session turning live delivery on, and nothing reaches the connection unless the session takes it, so this does not multiply the traffic of what was forwarded; one too large for the session is passed over instead. A push that was skipped (saturation, a cooldown) made no attempt, and its message goes with the grant's next push; pickup always works. A message that is not yet due is never pushed before it is due (the store does not hand it out, and the sink wakes nothing for it); once due it goes with the grant's next push, or is picked up. **What a live recipient can rely on:** while it has a live session with live delivery on, every due message that entered the store after the `status` that answered its `live-delivery-change` is pushed over that connection (to the newest such session, FR-PICK-06), oldest first, one at a time, as long as its pushes succeed, whatever the clocks do; one whose push fails, or that arrives during a cooldown, a saturation or a shutdown (below), stays queued for pickup and for the grant's next wake-up; messages that `status` counted are pickup-only; delivery is at-least-once: a pushed message stays leased to the session until `messages-received` and goes back to the queue if the session closes or the lease times out first — from then on only a `delivery-request` delivers it, never another live push (FR-PICK-06; owner decision 2026-11-20 on #100) — so a recipient in live mode must still ask for queued messages after a reconnect or a slow period (this library's live stream does, every `LivePollInterval`). **The deadline:** each take and its push have one `PushTimeout` deadline (default 30 s; at most 5 min) covering the take, the registration check, the send and the give-back or pass-over. At the deadline the worker stops waiting, counts `Push-Failed` and serves other grants, even when the session, the DID resolver or the store ignores cancellation; the abandoned call runs on, and its record stays under its lease until the call ends, when it is given back, passed over or left leased to the session (a session that ignored the cancellation and reports the message sent; the library's WebSocket session honours the cancellation and queues no frame once its token is cancelled). So an abandoned send may still reach the recipient, which may then get the message twice (delivery is at-least-once). **One operation per grant, until it settles:** a grant has at most one operation from its start until the work it started has actually ended — not until its deadline — keyed by grant, so a new session does not reset it; a wake-up meanwhile is held and served after it. **Fair and bounded:** `PushConcurrency` workers (default 4; 1 to 64); the grants with a wake-up are served in turn, and an operation takes at most `MaxDeliveryBatch` records, then yields its worker and its grant takes its turn again, so a recipient that is slow to take a push (a DID that is slow to resolve, a session that is slow to take it) holds one worker at a time, never all; at most 2 × `PushConcurrency` operations exist between their start and the end of their work, abandoned ones included — a worker that takes a woken grant beyond that starts nothing (`Push-Skipped-Saturated`), and the grant's messages wait for pickup or its next wake-up. So a host collaborator — a return-route session, the DID resolver or the store — that never completes a call after it was cancelled keeps that call's operation, and its grant, unsettled until it does complete, and 2 × `PushConcurrency` such calls stop every push, for any recipient, until one of them completes; pickup keeps working throughout. Per grant that is woken, busy or cooling down the workers keep one small entry (its DID, at most 2 048 characters, a few flags and when its last cooldown started), dropped when it is none of these — that very entry only, so an entry a worker still holds after it was replaced never removes its replacement and lets a second operation of the grant start — and nothing of any message — never a forward's wire id, which the message model does not bound. **Cooldown:** a grant whose live push — in any step, the registration check included — reaches the deadline (a direct push's deadline starts the direct pause above instead) is paused for 2 × `PushTimeout` (one minute at the defaults), measured on the monotonic clock (`TimeProvider.GetTimestamp`), so a step of the wall clock neither lengthens nor shortens it: its wake-ups meanwhile end at once with `Push-Skipped-Cooldown` without taking a worker, and their messages wait for pickup or the grant's first wake-up after the pause. A peer that stalls its own pushes therefore holds a worker at most once every three push timeouts, not continuously. Residual: with K grants whose every push stalls until it ends at its deadline and W workers, a healthy recipient's push can wait about ceil(K / W) × `PushTimeout` for a worker, and once K ≥ 3 × W the stalled grants, each back after its cooldown, can keep every worker busy, so that wait recurs; nothing is lost and pickup always works; a mediation policy that enrols only known peers (an allow-list `IMediationPolicy`) closes it — grants are free under the default policy. A push that does not end at its deadline is the unsettled-call residual above. Forwards are outside replay admission (FR-RR-08), so each replay of a captured forward is stored again and, for a live recipient, pushed again — the recipient's quota bounds only how many copies are held at once, and an acknowledged push frees its place; recipients de-duplicate by message id. **The direct push** (#110, D6 step 3; `MediatorOptions.DirectPush`, off by default; read from the settings in effect, nothing is registered conditionally). Each push checks the live session first (live wins); only when the grant's peer has no live session with live delivery on does it make the direct step (`PickupCoordinator.PushNextDirectAsync`), under the same `PushTimeout` deadline and in this order: (a) the endpoint: `IRecipientEndpointDirectory.FindAsync(grantPeerDid, ct)` — the host's directory, registered before `AddMediator()`, keyed by the grant's peer DID (not by the recipient DID, which is known only after a take) and read before anything is taken; `null` or a throw is no endpoint (a throw is logged by its exception type only; a cancellation propagates); the directory may be asked about a DID that holds no grant, or no longer does, and may answer `null` or anything: nothing is sent for such a DID (nothing to take; a record still held under a revoked grant is removed as unregistered); (b) the endpoint checks, still before the take: an absolute `http` or `https` URI — so never `ws`/`wss` (a WebSocket gives no receipt, transports.md L77) nor the queue URI `didcomm:transport/queue` (return_route.md L15, "held at the sender for pickup") — whose scheme the HTTP transport's `AllowedSchemes` accepts — else no endpoint: `Push-Skipped-NoEndpoint` at an operation's first push, nothing taken, the operation ends and the messages wait for pickup; then the core outbound check (`DidCommOptions.OutboundEndpointPolicy`) with host names looked up asynchronously under the push deadline (red-team pass 2, the coordinator's call under the churn rule, L-063, not an owner decision): an endpoint it refuses (a private, loopback, link-local or metadata address, or a name that resolves to one or does not resolve) gets no POST: nothing is taken, `Push-Failed` is counted, and the recipient's direct pushes pause; behind a `TrustedProxy` each followed 307 hop's destination is checked the same way, direct connections through the default `UseHttpTransport()` handler check every address when the connection opens, and on a host whose own connection code replaced the library's the check before a push is a name check only and the name is looked up again at connect (OD-7, below); the log names the reason only, never the URI or a DID; (b′) live, once more: live or direct is decided right before the take, because the directory is host code that may take up to `PushTimeout` — a live session found then (one that turned live delivery on meanwhile, or a downgraded one that resumed) wins: nothing is taken and the next push of the operation goes live; a session that turns live delivery on after that, during the take or the send, finds the push in flight (like a revocation after the take), so a record its live-on `status` counted may also reach the endpoint — at most a duplicate, which recipients drop by message id (at-least-once); (c) the take: the grant's next record, chosen by the store as for every push above (`AfterSequence` unset, so FR-PICK-06 is untouched: a live session's mark matters only to a live push), under a push lease with no owner (no connection holds it; expiry `now + LeaseTimeout`, so a concurrent `delivery-request` stays off the record during the POST); the store sets the record's push mark in the same step, exactly as for a live push's take — **at most one push attempt per message, kept on the record**: `QueuedMessage.PushTaken` now means "a push, live or direct, took this record and has not said it was not sent: it is for a `delivery-request` only" (OD-3 and OD-5 of #100 applied to both pushes); a host store that implements the #100 contract needs no change; (d) a record that has expired by the send is given back unsent (mark cleared) and never sent, and the run ends, with no pause (so a store that judges expiry by a clock of its own costs at most one take per wake-up); (e) **one send** of the stored payload, unchanged (`application/didcomm-encrypted+json`: the mailbox holds JWEs only), to the endpoint read in (a), through the mediator's own `HttpDidCommTransport` instance — built with the transport's public constructor; a direct push sends through an `HttpClient` of the mediator's own over the innermost (primary) handler of the host's named `"didcomm"` client, taken from `IHttpClientFactory` for each push and kept until the send ends, with that client's own timeout (red-team 1a-1, the coordinator's call under the churn rule, L-063, not an owner decision): the handler `UseHttpTransport()` configures (the connect-time guard pin, no automatic redirects, direct by default, `TrustedProxy` only when the host names one); handlers in front of it — `ConfigureHttpClientDefaults` (for example .NET Aspire's standard resilience handler, with its retries and breaker), or the client's own retry, breaker, authentication or logging handlers, and the framework's request logging — do not apply to direct pushes — with no retry (`MaxRetryAttempts = 0`), a circuit breaker that never opens (`CircuitBreakerFailureThreshold = int.MaxValue`), the host's `MaxRedirectHops`, the host's outbound policy and every other setting copied; it is never registered as `IDidCommTransport`, so the host's own sends and the push share neither retries nor a breaker (one failing endpoint cannot stop other recipients' pushes or the host's sends); (f) a `2xx` at the end of the chain removes the record (`Pushed-Direct`); any other answer — a status outside `2xx`, a `301`/`302`/`303`/`308` (never followed), a `307` chain longer than `MaxRedirectHops`, a `307` without a usable `Location`, a hop the outbound guard refuses, a transport fault — releases the record with its mark kept (`Push-Failed`): it waits for a `delivery-request` and is never pushed again. A cancellation or the deadline during the send also keeps the mark. **An answered `4xx` other than `408` and `429`** (red-team 1a-4, the coordinator's call under the churn rule, L-063, not an owner decision): the endpoint's definite "not this message"; the operation moves on to the next record with no pause, so messages anyone stored ahead of a real one cannot hold it back, and a run yields unless it met a refusal and delivered nothing, so a real message waits behind refused ones only when a whole run of them (more than `MaxDeliveryBatch` in a row) delivers nothing; then until the next wake-up, or pickup (red-team 2b-1). **The direct pause** (the coordinator's call under the churn rule, L-063, not an owner decision): any other direct push that does not deliver — a `5xx`, `408` or `429`, a `3xx` at the end of the chain, a `307` chain past the bound, no answer, a send fault (Polly's and `HttpClient`'s own timeouts included), an endpoint the outbound check refused before the take, a fault in the step, or the deadline on the direct branch — ends its run (no next record, no yield) and pauses that recipient's direct pushes for 2 × `PushTimeout`, on the monotonic clock. Wake-ups during the pause still run the live step, so a live session that appears gets its pushes; only the direct branch is skipped (`Push-Skipped-Cooldown`, once per operation). A record found expired at send time ends the run with no pause; a live push's deadline keeps the cooldown below. So a refused message never holds up later ones (a `4xx`) or holds them only for the pause (anything else), and is never sent again; after a pause the ones behind it go at the first wake-up after the pause, until the next push that does not deliver (one run per pause; under an endpoint that keeps failing, part of them go only by pickup), or by pickup; a run yields unless it met a refusal and delivered nothing, so a real message waits behind refused ones only when a whole run of them (more than `MaxDeliveryBatch` in a row) delivers nothing; then until the next wake-up, or pickup. A store that drops the mark gets a `4xx`-refused record POSTed up to 2 × `MaxDeliveryBatch` − 1 times per wake-up (a run that delivered something ahead of it yields once more), and any other failed one at most once per pause. (It replaced the first build's "not delivered → next record", its per-run memory of taken ids and its longer request timeout for the push transport, which QA round 3 reopened; the push transport's `RequestTimeout` is the host's. Red-team 1a-4 brought the next record back for an answered `4xx` only.) A `2xx` whose removal then fails still counts as delivered (an Error line); the record keeps its mark and lease, so only a `delivery-request` gets it again. A session that closes during an operation hands the rest of the operation to the direct push; what was pushed live is not pushed again. The mediator's own log lines carry the status, a fixed reason or the exception type, never an exception message (it names the URI), a `Location`, a DID or a record id. The framework's `IHttpClientFactory` request logging (categories `System.Net.Http.HttpClient.didcomm.*`) sits in front of the innermost handler, so it does not see direct pushes; the host's own sends through the named client still write their addresses there. **Redirects (owner decision OD-1 for #110, 2026-11-22).** The question as put: "#110 direct push: the recipient's registered server answers "307 Temporary Redirect, post it over there". The DIDComm spec says senders SHOULD follow 307s, and our HTTP transport does. But on PR #109, following them let a push land at a server the host never registered and caused up to 24 sends of one message. What should a direct push do with a 307?" The owner's answer: "Follow 307, tightly bounded (Recommended)": only `307` (transports.md L69: "Only temporary redirects (307) are acceptable"), never `301`/`302`/`303`/`308`, up to the HTTP transport's existing bound `HttpTransportOptions.MaxRedirectHops` (default 5; no new setting), every new address through the outbound guard, the whole chain one attempt with no retry; a `2xx` at the end is delivered, anything else leaves the message for pickup with its attempt spent. Cost, as put: a push can end up at an address the recipient's own server chose, not the one the host registered (at most `MaxRedirectHops` + 1 POSTs per message, each vetted). **Failed transmission (owner decision OD-2 for #110, 2026-11-22).** The question as put: "#110 direct push: the spec says that when a send fails, the sender SHOULD try another address or try delivery later. Our design: a failed push isn't retried; the message stays in the mailbox and reaches the recipient when they next pick up. Does pickup count as "delivery later"?" The owner's answer: "Yes, pickup is later delivery (Recommended)" (routing.md L173). **On a host with its own connection code (owner decision OD-7 for #110, 2026-11-23).** The question as put began: "#110, direct push's address check has now broken for the third time (unpublished DidComm.Mediator)." and explained that only hosts that replaced the library's connection path are affected. The option as put: "Consistent with your OD-5 answer. DirectPush's docs say that on a host with its own connection code, the check before a push is a name check only, and the host's connection code must check addresses at connect time itself. No code change." The owner's answer: "Document it (Recommended)". So on a host whose own connection code replaced the library's (a connect callback that checks nothing, or its own primary handler), the check before a push is a name check only and the name is looked up again at connect: the host's connection code must check addresses at connect itself, for every connection a push makes, as OD-5 says for a transport built by hand; the default `UseHttpTransport()` path checks every address when the connection opens. **Start-up.** With direct push in effect, start-up fails without an `IRecipientEndpointDirectory` (refused when the push workers are built); when the `"didcomm"` HTTP client's innermost (primary) handler, the one a direct push sends through, would undo what the push relies on — an `HttpClientHandler` (it cannot check a connection's address when it connects), or a `SocketsHttpHandler` that follows redirects on its own, has no connect callback, or uses a proxy other than `TrustedProxy` — which is what a host without `UseHttpTransport()` has; with an invalid `TrustedProxy`; or with `DidCommOptions.OutboundEndpointPolicy` set to `null`. A host that wraps the HTTP transport in its own decorator keeps `UseHttpTransport()`'s client and is accepted; any other primary handler is the host's own connection path (the residual below). Outside a generic host the same refusals come later: a missing directory when the push workers are first built (with the first relayed message), the rest at each direct push, which then fails (`Push-Failed`) and leaves the message for pickup. While direct push is off nothing builds the sender or resolves the directory. **With re-wrapping (owner decision OD-3 for #110, 2026-11-22).** In re-wrap mode the stored payload is the mediator's own anoncrypted `forward` addressed to the recipient (routing.md L71). The question as put: "#110: in "rewrap" mode, the mediator wraps each message one extra time, addressed to the recipient. The spec (routing.md L71) says receivers must expect this. Our published plain receiving endpoint (MapDidCommEndpoint in DidComm.AspNetCore) doesn't unwrap it: it answers 202 and the message is never read. So a direct push in rewrap mode would get a message deleted unread. The same happens today with any rewrap-mode mediator delivering to our endpoint. What should #110 do?" The owner's answer: "Fix the endpoint in this PR (Recommended)". The plain receiving endpoints now open such a forward (FR-ROUTE-06, receiver side at a plain endpoint), so direct push works in either `RewrapMode`; the start-up refusal of the combination that was first built (ours) is gone. **Residuals (stated):** a tarpitting endpoint holds a worker for at most `PushTimeout` once per direct pause, whatever ends the stall (the fairness rules above apply unchanged), and an endpoint answering each push just before the deadline can hold one worker for up to `MaxDeliveryBatch` × `PushTimeout` per wake-up, as a slow accepting one always could (red-team 2a-3); a host store that drops the mark re-pushes such a record up to 2 × `MaxDeliveryBatch` − 1 times per wake-up (a run that delivered something ahead of it yields once more) when the endpoint refuses it with a `4xx` and at most once per pause otherwise, and a crash between the take and the send leaves the record marked (pickup only); a session that turns live delivery on after the direct take finds that push in flight (at most a duplicate); delivery stays at-least-once (a deadline or a dropped connection after the receiver handled the POST, a `2xx` whose removal fails, a POST that outlives its lease); a revocation or acknowledgement after the take's registration check lets the POST in flight complete; a host that replaced the `"didcomm"` client's primary handler, or whose connect callback is its own, owns its connect, redirect and proxy behaviour: the check before the take is a name check only and the name is looked up again at connect, so that connection code must check addresses at connect itself (OD-5, OD-7); "live wins" is decided by the sessions of the process running the push (red-team 1b-3, docs only): with two mediator processes over one store, a process with no live session for a grant may POST a message the other process's live session would have taken (FR-PICK-06 applies per process), and the push mark still prevents duplicates and loss; a directory that ignores cancellation keeps its operation unsettled (the unsettled-call residual above); and a recipient that relies on the push still picks up: a refused push and a delayed message wait for the next wake-up or pickup (D2 of the "after #95" decisions, OD-2). **Outcomes** on `didcomm.mediator.push.outcomes` (tag `didcomm.push.outcome`), one per push attempt (a take and its push over the live session or to the registered endpoint; a fault or the deadline while taking counts as `Push-Failed`) and one per wake-up that was skipped or could push nothing it had: `Pushed-Live`, `Pushed-Direct` (the registered endpoint answered `2xx`; the record is removed), `Push-Failed`, `Push-Skipped-Saturated`, `Push-Skipped-NoLiveSession` (direct push off), `Push-Skipped-NoEndpoint` (direct push on, no live session and no usable endpoint, at an operation's first push; nothing taken), `Push-Skipped-Unregistered`, `Push-Skipped-Cooldown` (the grant cooling down after a live push's deadline, or, with direct push, a wake-up whose live step found no session while that recipient's direct pushes are paused, once per operation) or `Push-Skipped-Shutdown` (a wake-up after shutdown, a waiting one it dropped, or a push it cancelled; every message stays queued). | `DeliverAsync` with live push allowed: live session present → the payload is admitted to the store, pushed, and held leased until `messages-received` (retained count +1 until the ack, 0 after); no session → queued; a DID no grant has registered → not retained. The sink: a registered recipient's message is stored, counted `Queued` once and then pushed live by the push workers; the sink stores and returns while a live push is stuck, and other grants' messages go on; a message not yet due is never pushed live; behind a stuck push the grant has one operation, every message the queue stores is pushed once the push moves, and the queue's quota is the only limit; while a push is blocked the dispatcher retains the grant's DID and nothing of the forwards, whatever the forward id's length; an unregistered recipient, a `next` that is not a DID, an expired, too large or over-quota forward and a queue fault are each dropped and counted under their own outcome; a dropped forward wakes no push; a cancellation of the handler's token before anything is stored leaves as cancellation and counts nothing (`MediatorForwardSinkTests`); the dispatcher, driven through live sessions (`LivePushDispatcherTests`): a message not yet due is not pushed, a payload of any admissible size is taken when idle, a wake-up whose peer has no live session counts that once and takes nothing, a store that fails to take the record is a failure and nothing is sent, a take that ignores the deadline frees the worker and its record is given back unsent when it returns, a registration check that throws is a failure and never a push and one that names another peer's grant is unregistered, a give-back that ignores the cancellation frees the worker at the deadline and holds its grant until it ends, a cancellation callback that throws cannot escape the push deadline, with every operation unsettled a wake-up starts nothing and the next one after they settle is served, one grant never has two pushes in flight while grants together use every worker, `Wake` takes a bounded grant DID and the dispatcher keeps nothing else of a message, disposing while a push holds a throwing cancellation callback neither throws nor loses a message, the workers do not inherit the execution context of whoever woke them, and a dispatcher built inside a suppressed flow still starts its workers; over the real endpoints a live session takes the message first, after it closes the next message is queued for pickup, a session aborted around the push never loses the message, of two live sessions each message goes to exactly one (the newest, then the older once the newest closes), a session that stops reading takes what its push bound allows and the rest is queued, never both, and a live push left unacknowledged on a session that closes is fetchable from the other session (`MediatorDeliveryModesQaTests`); the dispatcher's invariants: records that expired, were acknowledged or were picked up are never pushed and never hold up a due one (D-1), wake-ups of a grant waiting for a worker coalesce into one operation and one that arrives after the operation last read the store is served right after it (D-2), a grant gets no second operation while its abandoned push runs, even on a new session (D-3), and every push ends the lease it took exactly once — a live push sent and one not sent (D-5) (`PushDispatcherInvariantTests`); a lease request with `AfterSequence` selects only the records with a greater arrival sequence and marks no others, and every status reports the highest sequence assigned (`MessagePickupStoreSequenceTests`); a record without an arrival sequence is refused, given back and still picked up (`PushArrivalOrderTests`); the ADV-5 break-it pass on the redesign: a message stored for a live recipient is pushed live after a backward clock step, and when its delivery read the clock before live delivery went on (defects 1 and 2); a store that ignores `AfterSequence` is refused; a stale grant entry never lets a second operation of the same grant start (defect 5), nor does revoking a peer and granting it again while its abandoned push runs; with 2 × `PushConcurrency` grants whose pushes never end no other grant is pushed, however long it waits (the stated residual); a busy grant yields its worker after `MaxDeliveryBatch` pushes; a message whose wake-up was skipped in a cooldown is pushed by the next wake-up, oldest first and not before; a backward or forward step of the wall clock neither lengthens nor shortens a cooldown (defect 6) (`PushDispatcherAdv5Tests`); the ADV-6 break-it pass on the arrival sequence: of two live sessions of one peer each message is pushed to one of them once while its lease holds, a session that never takes a push costs one send attempt per wake-up and leaves every message to pickup, a record whose recipient was unregistered is removed without an attempt and the next one is pushed, and a host store that numbers nothing pushes nothing while pickup still answers (`PushDispatcherAdv6Tests`); a store that never returns from the take holds a worker only until the push deadline, a push in flight holds its record against a pickup and gives it back the moment it fails, and disposing the container with a push in flight throws nothing and loses nothing (`MediatorLifetimeAdvTests`); a replayed captured forward is bounded by the recipient's quota when nothing is pushed (`MediatorEgressAdvTests`); live push: the worker takes what entered the store after the live-on `status`, oldest first, under the session's own lease, and what that `status` counted stays for pickup; a push the session does not take, or that throws, gives the lease back at once and ends the operation, and is tried again at the next wake-up; a message too large for the session's envelope is passed over, as a pickup does, and the next one is pushed; one that outlasts the deadline (whether or not the session honours the cancellation; one that ignores it frees the worker and gives the lease back once its send ends), a session that closes during the push and a shutdown with a push in flight all give the lease back; a record a pickup took first is not pushed; of two live sessions the newest gets the push; the sink wakes nothing when the peer is not live; a grant whose live push reaches the deadline — the registration check included — is paused, its wake-ups never take a worker, everything stays fetchable and pushes resume after the cooldown, and a live push that ignores its deadline keeps its grant waiting while a wake-up held meanwhile falls in the cooldown; a failure before the deadline pauses nothing; the grant entries drop the cooldowns that ran out (`LivePushTests`); a grant whose live push never returns holds up neither the sink nor another grant's live push; with every worker held by a stalled grant another live recipient is pushed within one push timeout; a lease recorded after the session's close-time release is given back at once; a pickup that takes the record during the live push's take wins and nothing is sent twice; an acknowledgement during the push, and a revocation and purge while a wake-up waits (even with the peer granted again), leave nothing behind; an abandoned send that still goes out is not delivered again within its lease and returns to pickup when the lease ends, and one that reports sent after its session closed is given back by id and stays marked, so it returns to pickup only (#100); a recipient that never acknowledges and polls elsewhere once per lease period gets every message; with a session that takes every push, a burst behind a slow first push loses nothing, keeps order and counts one outcome per message, and every message that arrives while live delivery is on is pushed; the dispatcher retains bounded values for a forward with a hostile id; live sends that ignore their cancellation never pile up: through eight cycles of deadline, session close and reopen, cooldown expiry, pickup and acknowledgement at most one send is pending, and still counted, while a healthy recipient keeps getting its pushes and the stalled one its pickups (`Review_round_2_live_sends_that_ignore_cancellation_never_pile_up_across_deadlines_session_churn_and_cooldowns`, the PR #109 review's round-2 finding 2: on `755c497` one more send was pending after each cycle); a host's own live delivery alongside the workers delivers each message once; disposing the container while a live push ignores its cancellation returns promptly and loses nothing (`LivePushAdv4Tests`). Over the real endpoints, with two grants and one live recipient whose key resolution stalls, the other recipient's message is stored and picked up while the stall lasts, and a live push whose resolution outlasts the push timeout is abandoned and its message is fetchable (`MediatorLiveIsolationTests`); on a session with push room for the whole burst, a burst behind a slow first push loses nothing and later messages are pushed again, and every message that arrives while live delivery is on reaches the live connection (a session whose push slots are full does not take a push: `Push-Failed`, and the message waits for the grant's next wake-up or for pickup, as the retry rule above says); with every worker held by grants whose resolution stalls, another live recipient is pushed once their push timeout ends, and after that round the stalled grants are paused and its next push goes out at once; grants whose resolution stalls cannot make the workers refuse another live recipient's push; an expired message queued ahead never costs a later message its live push (`Review_round_2_an_expired_message_queued_ahead_never_costs_a_later_message_its_live_push`, the PR #109 review's round-2 finding 1: on `755c497` the third message was refused as `Push-Skipped-Saturated` and never pushed); a message that ages out while the grant's first live push is held is never pushed; a live push abandoned at the deadline is given back to pickup and never written later, even when the resolution ignores cancellation (`MediatorLivePushAdv4Tests`). The re-check: a delivery parked between its lookup and its store while the grant is revoked and purged removes its own record and reports `NotRegistered`, and a live session gets no push for it; the same peer granted again meanwhile keeps the message; a re-check that fails keeps the record, reports `Queued` and pushes nothing live (`PickupRevokeAndPurgeTests`). The direct push (#110): the step posts exactly the stored bytes once to the registered endpoint and a `2xx` removes the record; an attempt that does not deliver releases the lease, keeps the mark, and only a `delivery-request` gets the record again; without a usable endpoint nothing is taken; an unregistered recipient's record is removed unsent; a record that expired, aged out, was acknowledged, or was purged and re-granted before its take is never sent, nor one that expires between its take and its send (Q-1); a deadline during the take sends nothing and gives the record back unsent, one during the send keeps the mark; the step leaves its caller before any host code runs; the POST goes to the endpoint read before the take; a record one push took is never taken by the other kind; a refused record is posted once and never blocks later messages (ADV-5 D3/D4); after a restart over the same store no attempted record is posted again (ADV-6 D1); a host store that hands back a taken record is refused; a `2xx` whose removal fails is still delivered and kept from pushes; no log line carries a DID, a record id, an endpoint or an exception message (`DirectPushStepTests`); a push lease without an owner marks what it takes, no connection's close ends it and only its own "not sent" clears the mark, and one mark serves both pushes (`MessagePickupStoreLivePushReturnTests`); the sender's settings copy names every public transport setting and keeps the host's except no retry, a breaker that never opens and the resolved policy, Polly accepts that breaker (a hundred failures in a row each reach the endpoint), the endpoint checks refuse with fixed reasons and use the policy `SendPackedAsync` uses, the request is the stored bytes as an encrypted message with no return route, a non-`2xx` logs only its status, and `Create` refuses a container without the HTTP transport, an invalid `TrustedProxy` and a null core policy (`DirectPushSenderTests`); the dispatcher posts a due message of a grant without a live session unchanged and removes it, goes on past a refused message, skips a grant without an endpoint once per operation, removes an unregistered recipient's record unposted, counts nothing for one that expired between take and send, fails each direct push of a sender that cannot be built without stopping a worker, posts a delayed message only once due (D2), lets a live session win and not ask the directory (D6), hands the rest of an operation to direct push when the session closes mid-operation, holds one worker per tarpitting endpoint while every other grant is pushed in turn (Q-4), keeps nothing of a waiting forward's wire id (R1-1), and posts each record at most once with more grants than `MaxGrants` on a host store (ADV-6 D1) (`DirectPushDispatcherTests`); off by default nothing of direct push is built; on, a missing directory, a missing HTTP transport and an invalid trusted proxy fail start-up (outside a host: when the push workers are built, or each direct push fails while the worker goes on);
